q-0001simple3I have an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass and I just moved to a new apartment last week. How soon do I need to update MOM with my new address, and how do I do it?You need to update MOM with your new residential address **within 2 weeks** of moving. Here's how to do it: 1. Make sure your new address meets the relevant housing requirements 2. Log in to **EP eService** using your Singpass 3. Update your address or mobile number – the update is immediate 4. Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card Since you moved last week, you have about one more week to complete this update. It's best to do it soon, as you may be penalised if you don't update within the 2-week deadline.The chatbot's answer covers all essential points: 2-week deadline, penalty warning, housing requirements check, EP eService login, immediate update, and printing acknowledgement page. The additional mention of Singpass and mobile number is minor extra detail, not incorrect.
Question:
I have an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass and I just moved to a new apartment last week. How soon do I need to update MOM with my new address, and how do I do it?
Expected:
You need to update MOM within 2 weeks of changing your residential address. Otherwise, you may be penalised. To update: (1) Ensure that your new address meets the relevant housing requirements. (2) Log in to EP eService to update your address. The update is immediate. (3) Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card.
Actual:
You need to update MOM with your new residential address **within 2 weeks** of moving.

Here's how to do it:

1. Make sure your new address meets the relevant housing requirements
2. Log in to **EP eService** using your Singpass
3. Update your address or mobile number – the update is immediate
4. Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card

Since you moved last week, you have about one more week to complete this update. It's best to do it soon, as you may be penalised if you don't update within the 2-week deadline.
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer covers all essential points: 2-week deadline, penalty warning, housing requirements check, EP eService login, immediate update, and printing acknowledgement page. The additional mention of Singpass and mobile number is minor extra detail, not incorrect.

q-0002simple3We have an EP holder on an EP (Sponsorship) whose pass is expiring soon. I want to make sure I submit the renewal in time — how early can I start the renewal, and how long should I expect processing to take? Also, once we get the IPA letter, how long is it valid for?For an EP (Sponsorship) renewal, you can start the application **3 months before the pass expires**. Processing takes **around 6 weeks** for most cases. Once you receive the IPA letter after approval, it's valid for **3 months**. You must get the pass issued before either the IPA expires or the current pass expires, whichever comes first. A few other points to keep in mind: - Any remaining validity from the current pass (up to 6 months) will be added to the renewed pass, so you won't lose time by renewing early - The renewed pass can be issued for up to 3 years - You'll need to use the EP (Sponsorship) renewal application form and submit it through the online form Can I help with anything else about the renewal process?The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 3-month early renewal window, ~6 weeks processing time, 3-month IPA validity, the requirement to issue before IPA or current pass expires, remaining validity being added, and up to 3-year renewal duration. The answer is comprehensive and substantively equivalent to the reference.
Question:
We have an EP holder on an EP (Sponsorship) whose pass is expiring soon. I want to make sure I submit the renewal in time — how early can I start the renewal, and how long should I expect processing to take? Also, once we get the IPA letter, how long is it valid for?
Expected:
For EP (Sponsorship), you can start applying to renew 3 months before the pass expires (as opposed to the standard EP, which allows renewal up to 6 months / 180 days before expiry). You must apply before the expiry date. Processing time for EP (Sponsorship) renewals is within 6 weeks for most cases (the renewal form itself states around 8 weeks). Once the renewal is approved, you will receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for 3 months. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or the current pass expires, whichever occurs first — otherwise an overstaying fine will be imposed. The duration of the renewed pass will start when the current pass expires. Any remaining validity from the current pass (up to six months) is added on to the renewal, so renewing early does not reduce or lose validity. The renewed pass can be granted for up to 3 years.
Actual:
For an EP (Sponsorship) renewal, you can start the application **3 months before the pass expires**. 

Processing takes **around 6 weeks** for most cases.

Once you receive the IPA letter after approval, it's valid for **3 months**. You must get the pass issued before either the IPA expires or the current pass expires, whichever comes first.

A few other points to keep in mind:

- Any remaining validity from the current pass (up to 6 months) will be added to the renewed pass, so you won't lose time by renewing early
- The renewed pass can be issued for up to 3 years
- You'll need to use the EP (Sponsorship) renewal application form and submit it through the online form

Can I help with anything else about the renewal process?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 3-month early renewal window, ~6 weeks processing time, 3-month IPA validity, the requirement to issue before IPA or current pass expires, remaining validity being added, and up to 3-year renewal duration. The answer is comprehensive and substantively equivalent to the reference.

q-0003simple3I'm looking into the Employment Pass for a candidate we want to hire in Singapore. What's the minimum salary needed to qualify? The minimum salary needed for an Employment Pass depends on the candidate's age and the sector they'll be working in. **Current requirements (until 31 December 2026):** For **general sectors** (excluding financial services): - **$5,600/month** for candidates aged 23 or below - Increases progressively with age up to **$10,700/month** at age 45 and above For the **financial services sector**: - **$6,200/month** for candidates aged 23 or below - Increases progressively with age up to **$11,800/month** at age 45 and above **From 1 January 2027** (new applications) and **1 January 2028** (renewals), the thresholds will increase: For **general sectors**: - **$6,000/month** for candidates aged 23 or below - Up to **$11,500/month** at age 45 and above For **financial services**: - **$6,600/month** for candidates aged 23 or below - Up to **$12,700/month** at age 45 and above Keep in mind that meeting the qualifying salary is only **Stage 1**. Unless your candidate earns at least **$22,500/month** (which exempts them), they'll also need to pass the **COMPASS points-based assessment** (Stage 2), scoring at least 40 points across criteria like salary benchmarking, qualifications, diversity, and support for local employment. Can I help with anything else about the Employment Pass?The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, covering the key points from the reference (starting salary of $5,600, progressive increase with age up to $10,700 for mid-40s, higher thresholds for financial services) while providing additional useful details about future changes and the COMPASS assessment.
Question:
I'm looking into the Employment Pass for a candidate we want to hire in Singapore. What's the minimum salary needed to qualify?
Expected:
The qualifying salary for an Employment Pass is a fixed monthly salary comparable to the top one-third of local PMET salaries. It starts from $5,600 and increases progressively with age, up to $10,700 for candidates in their mid-40s. Candidates in the financial services sector need higher salaries than these amounts to qualify. The actual value varies with age and sector of employment.
Actual:
The minimum salary needed for an Employment Pass depends on the candidate's age and the sector they'll be working in.

**Current requirements (until 31 December 2026):**

For **general sectors** (excluding financial services):
- **$5,600/month** for candidates aged 23 or below
- Increases progressively with age up to **$10,700/month** at age 45 and above

For the **financial services sector**:
- **$6,200/month** for candidates aged 23 or below  
- Increases progressively with age up to **$11,800/month** at age 45 and above

**From 1 January 2027** (new applications) and **1 January 2028** (renewals), the thresholds will increase:

For **general sectors**:
- **$6,000/month** for candidates aged 23 or below
- Up to **$11,500/month** at age 45 and above

For **financial services**:
- **$6,600/month** for candidates aged 23 or below
- Up to **$12,700/month** at age 45 and above

Keep in mind that meeting the qualifying salary is only **Stage 1**. Unless your candidate earns at least **$22,500/month** (which exempts them), they'll also need to pass the **COMPASS points-based assessment** (Stage 2), scoring at least 40 points across criteria like salary benchmarking, qualifications, diversity, and support for local employment.

Can I help with anything else about the Employment Pass?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, covering the key points from the reference (starting salary of $5,600, progressive increase with age up to $10,700 for mid-40s, higher thresholds for financial services) while providing additional useful details about future changes and the COMPASS assessment.

q-0004simple3We have a candidate who's been approved for an ONE Pass. His common-law partner wants to come along and eventually work here. What type of pass does the common-law spouse get, and can they work in Singapore on that pass?The common-law partner of an ONE Pass holder qualifies for a **Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)**. Yes, they can work in Singapore on that pass. The common-law spouse must apply for a **Letter of Consent (LOC)** through their prospective employer. The employer completes the LOC application form and submits it online on behalf of the common-law spouse. Processing takes around 8 weeks, and the LTVP must have at least 3 months' validity before applying. Other LTVP holders who are not common-law spouses would need to obtain a separate work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) instead. Can I help with anything else regarding the ONE Pass or family passes?The chatbot correctly identifies LTVP for the common-law partner and LOC for work authorization, matching the reference answer. Additional details about the application process are helpful, not misleading.
Question:
We have a candidate who's been approved for an ONE Pass. His common-law partner wants to come along and eventually work here. What type of pass does the common-law spouse get, and can they work in Singapore on that pass?
Expected:
A common-law spouse (defined as a common-law relationship between 2 people that is recognised under the laws of their country or region) of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder is eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP). Common-law spouses on LTVP can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore.
Actual:
The common-law partner of an ONE Pass holder qualifies for a **Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)**.

Yes, they can work in Singapore on that pass. The common-law spouse must apply for a **Letter of Consent (LOC)** through their prospective employer. The employer completes the LOC application form and submits it online on behalf of the common-law spouse. Processing takes around 8 weeks, and the LTVP must have at least 3 months' validity before applying.

Other LTVP holders who are not common-law spouses would need to obtain a separate work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) instead.

Can I help with anything else regarding the ONE Pass or family passes?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot correctly identifies LTVP for the common-law partner and LOC for work authorization, matching the reference answer. Additional details about the application process are helpful, not misleading.

q-0005simple2I'm an employer planning to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate. How long does it take to process the application, and how much is the application fee?The Employment Pass application is processed **within 10 business days** when you apply online through the standard EP eService route. The **application fee is $105**. If you're applying for an overseas company without a Singapore-registered entity, the processing time is longer — **within 6 weeks** for most cases — and you'll need to use the EP Sponsorship route with a local sponsor. Can I help you with anything else about the application, such as eligibility requirements or what documents you'll need?Covers key points (10 business days for standard, 6 weeks for sponsorship, $105 fee) but omits payment method details and the caveat that processing may take longer if additional documents are required.
Question:
I'm an employer planning to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate. How long does it take to process the application, and how much is the application fee?
Expected:
The processing time and fee depend on whether the employer has a Singapore-registered company or not. For employers with a Singapore-registered company applying online via the EP eService, the application is processed or given an update within 10 business days, and the application fee is $105, payable by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex. For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company (sponsorship cases), a local sponsor must submit the application, the processing time is within 6 weeks for most cases (some cases may take longer), and the application fee is also $105, payable by PayNow, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex. In either case, if additional information or documents are required, the processing time may take longer than the stated timeframes.
Actual:
The Employment Pass application is processed **within 10 business days** when you apply online through the standard EP eService route.

The **application fee is $105**.

If you're applying for an overseas company without a Singapore-registered entity, the processing time is longer — **within 6 weeks** for most cases — and you'll need to use the EP Sponsorship route with a local sponsor.

Can I help you with anything else about the application, such as eligibility requirements or what documents you'll need?
Judge remark (scored 2):
Covers key points (10 business days for standard, 6 weeks for sponsorship, $105 fee) but omits payment method details and the caveat that processing may take longer if additional documents are required.

q-0006simple2We have a candidate who wants to apply for the ONE Pass, but our company is not publicly listed. Can we use our company valuation to qualify as an established company, or is there another way for non-listed companies to meet the criteria?Looking at my answer, I stated: 1. US$200 million annual revenue requirement 2. S$30,000 fixed monthly salary for 12 consecutive months Both of these figures appear in the loaded packet content: 1. **US$200 million**: From the FAQ "Can a non-publicly listed company be considered established company based on company valuation instead of market capitalisation?" which states: "Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least US$200 million to be considered established companies as they do not have market capitalisation." 2. **S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months**: From the FAQ "Does my prospective employer need to meet the criteria for an established company?" which states: "If not, you may re-apply for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass after you have earned a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for the12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application." Both figures came directly from the loaded packet content, not from the catalog or my prior knowledge. --- No, company valuation cannot be used to qualify as an established company for the ONE Pass. Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least **US$200 million** to be considered established companies. Since they don't have market capitalisation, the revenue threshold is the only way for non-listed companies to meet the established company requirement. If your company doesn't meet this revenue threshold, your candidate has two other options to qualify for the ONE Pass: 1. **Salary-based route**: Apply after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months from any one employer (the employer doesn't need to be an established company in this case) 2. **Outstanding achievements route**: Qualify based on outstanding achievements in sports, arts and culture, or academia and research Can I help you with anything else about the ONE Pass application?Correctly states valuation is not accepted, gives the US$200M revenue criterion, and mentions the S$30,000 salary route. However, it omits the alternative work passes (EP, PEP, EntrePass, Tech.Pass) mentioned in the reference and adds an 'outstanding achievements' route not in the reference. Also includes extraneous internal reasoning text.
Question:
We have a candidate who wants to apply for the ONE Pass, but our company is not publicly listed. Can we use our company valuation to qualify as an established company, or is there another way for non-listed companies to meet the criteria?
Expected:
No, company valuation is not accepted as an alternative to market capitalisation. Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least US$200 million to be considered established companies, as they do not have market capitalisation. If the prospective employer does not meet the established company criteria, the candidate may still apply for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application. Alternatively, the candidate may apply for another work pass such as the Employment Pass, Personalised Employment Pass, EntrePass, or Tech.Pass.
Actual:
Looking at my answer, I stated:

1. US$200 million annual revenue requirement
2. S$30,000 fixed monthly salary for 12 consecutive months

Both of these figures appear in the loaded packet content:

1. **US$200 million**: From the FAQ "Can a non-publicly listed company be considered established company based on company valuation instead of market capitalisation?" which states: "Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least US$200 million to be considered established companies as they do not have market capitalisation."

2. **S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months**: From the FAQ "Does my prospective employer need to meet the criteria for an established company?" which states: "If not, you may re-apply for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass after you have earned a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for the12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application."

Both figures came directly from the loaded packet content, not from the catalog or my prior knowledge.

---

No, company valuation cannot be used to qualify as an established company for the ONE Pass.

Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least **US$200 million** to be considered established companies. Since they don't have market capitalisation, the revenue threshold is the only way for non-listed companies to meet the established company requirement.

If your company doesn't meet this revenue threshold, your candidate has two other options to qualify for the ONE Pass:

1. **Salary-based route**: Apply after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months from any one employer (the employer doesn't need to be an established company in this case)

2. **Outstanding achievements route**: Qualify based on outstanding achievements in sports, arts and culture, or academia and research

Can I help you with anything else about the ONE Pass application?
Judge remark (scored 2):
Correctly states valuation is not accepted, gives the US$200M revenue criterion, and mentions the S$30,000 salary route. However, it omits the alternative work passes (EP, PEP, EntrePass, Tech.Pass) mentioned in the reference and adds an 'outstanding achievements' route not in the reference. Also includes extraneous internal reasoning text.

q-0007simple3We're applying for an Employment Pass for a candidate whose qualification verification through a background screening company has been pending for over two weeks. The candidate's university is one of the institutions listed in the application form's drop-down list. Do we have to wait for the verification to come back before we can submit the EP application, or is there some workaround?Good news — you don't have to wait. If your verification has been pending for more than 14 working days **and** the candidate's university is in the application form's drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit the **receipt for the verification service** instead of the full verification proof. Once the application is approved, you can bring the candidate in and get the pass issued without needing to submit the verification proof to MOM. However, if the qualification is later found to be false, the work pass will be revoked and the candidate must leave Singapore. Question:
We have a candidate who earned S$30,000/month overseas for the past 12 months at a company with annual revenue of US$180 million and market cap of US$450 million. The company's global offices combined have annual revenue exceeding US$200 million though. Does the company qualify as established, and what documents do we need to submit for this candidate?
Expected:
For a company to be considered established, it must have at least one of the following: market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or annual revenue of at least US$200 million. Combined amounts from the entire global office can also be considered and will be assessed on a case-by-case basis. So while the individual office does not meet either threshold on its own, the combined global revenue exceeding US$200 million may qualify, but this will be assessed case-by-case by MOM.

For a candidate whose current or last held employment was overseas, the following documents are required:
1. Passport page (including any amendments). If the candidate's name in their passport differs from their name in other documents, an explanation letter and supporting documents such as a deed poll are also needed.
2. Monthly payslips for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application.
3. Employment verification proof from a background screening company.
4. Documents supporting other income sources other than fixed monthly salary (if applicable).
5. Latest financial information of the candidate's employer obtained within the last 3 months, including documents indicating the latest market capitalisation or annual company revenue. If this information is not available, the company's latest audited Profit & Loss statements may be provided instead. MOM will only consider documents from third party auditors; documents signed by the company's management staff will not be accepted.

Additionally, if the candidate drew salaries from more than one employer in the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application, the qualifying fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 (or its equivalent in foreign currency) should come from one employer only. Other sources of income may be considered on a case-by-case basis.
Actual:
Yes, **the company qualifies as established**. Combined amounts from the entire global office can be considered, and since your company's global offices together exceed US$200 million in annual revenue, it meets the established company requirement even though the individual entity falls short.

For your candidate applying based on **overseas employment**, you need to submit:

1. **Monthly payslips** for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the application date

2. **Employment verification proof** from a background screening company

3. **Latest financial information** of the employer obtained within the last 3 months showing:
   - Market capitalisation (if publicly listed), OR
   - Annual company revenue
   
   You can get this from:
   - Bloomberg
   - Reuters
   - Company's investor relations website
   - Stock exchange filings
   
   If this information isn't available, provide the company's latest audited Profit & Loss statements from a third-party auditor (not signed by company management)

4. **Passport page** including any amendments

5. **Documents supporting other income sources** (if applicable, beyond the fixed monthly salary)

Since you're relying on combined global revenue, make sure the financial documentation clearly shows the consolidated global figures exceeding US$200 million.

Can I help with anything else regarding this application?

Covers all key documents and correctly notes global revenue may qualify, but states definitively 'the company qualifies' instead of noting it will be assessed case-by-case. Also omits the detail about needing an explanation letter if passport name differs from other documents.

q-0009simple3We want to appoint an EP holder from another company to our Board of Directors. How long does the approval process take, what documents do we need, and how long is the approval valid for?The approval process takes **within 5 weeks**. **Documents required:** - Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship **Validity:** The Letter of Consent (LOC) is valid up to the expiry or cancellation date of the EP holder's current Employment Pass. When the EP is renewed, you'll need to apply for a new LOC. **Important requirements:** Before applying, you must ensure: 1. **Corporate relationship exists** – Your company must be related by shareholding to the EP holder's primary employer (reflected in ACRA records). This includes: - Direct shareholding relationships - Indirect relationships (e.g., holding companies or subsidiaries of the same holding company) 2. **Primary employer consent** – The EP holder's current employer has no objections to them taking on the directorship 3. **Related to primary employment** – The directorship must be for purposes related to their primary employment Once the LOC is approved, you can then register the directorship with ACRA. If your company is **not** related by shareholding to the EP holder's employer, you'll need support from a relevant sector government agency to have the application considered. Can I help with anything else regarding this appointment?The chatbot accurately covers all key points from the reference (5 weeks processing time, appointment letter document, LOC validity tied to EP expiry, need to reapply upon EP renewal) and adds relevant supplementary information about eligibility requirements.
Question:
We want to appoint an EP holder from another company to our Board of Directors. How long does the approval process take, what documents do we need, and how long is the approval valid for?
Expected:
The process takes within 5 weeks. The document required is the Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship. The validity of the Letter of Consent (LOC) is up to the expiry or cancellation date of the EP holder's current Employment Pass. When the EP is successfully renewed, you need to apply for a new LOC.
Actual:
The approval process takes **within 5 weeks**.

**Documents required:**
- Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship

**Validity:**
The Letter of Consent (LOC) is valid up to the expiry or cancellation date of the EP holder's current Employment Pass. When the EP is renewed, you'll need to apply for a new LOC.

**Important requirements:**

Before applying, you must ensure:

1. **Corporate relationship exists** – Your company must be related by shareholding to the EP holder's primary employer (reflected in ACRA records). This includes:
   - Direct shareholding relationships
   - Indirect relationships (e.g., holding companies or subsidiaries of the same holding company)

2. **Primary employer consent** – The EP holder's current employer has no objections to them taking on the directorship

3. **Related to primary employment** – The directorship must be for purposes related to their primary employment

Once the LOC is approved, you can then register the directorship with ACRA.

If your company is **not** related by shareholding to the EP holder's employer, you'll need support from a relevant sector government agency to have the application considered.

Can I help with anything else regarding this appointment?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately covers all key points from the reference (5 weeks processing time, appointment letter document, LOC validity tied to EP expiry, need to reapply upon EP renewal) and adds relevant supplementary information about eligibility requirements.

What are the eligibility requirements for the candidate under the SOL for that role?For a **carbon credit trader** to qualify for the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus under the Shortage Occupation List, the candidate must meet the following eligibility requirements: ## Job Duties The candidate must perform these key duties: - Trade or broker carbon credits - Originate and structure carbon credit deals - Facilitate transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits - Perform carbon market research and analysis on: - Different types of carbon credits and their attributes (project type, region, co-benefits, crediting mechanism) - Quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework) - Price dynamics in over-the-counter markets or exchanges - International regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6), as well as global and national compliance schemes ## Minimum Requirements The candidate **must** meet **both** of these criteria: 1. **Minimum three years' total work experience** in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields 2. **At least a degree-equivalent qualification** (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2 Qualifications criterion) ## Additional Assessment Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these minimum criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. ## Application Requirements - Select "Carbon trader" as the job title in the EP application form - This must match the occupation advertised on MyCareersFuture (unless you're exempt from FCF advertising requirements) Can I help you with anything else about the EP application process or COMPASS requirements?The chatbot's answer covers all essential eligibility requirements: the two minimum criteria (3 years experience in carbon markets, degree-equivalent qualification), job duties, EnterpriseSG assessment, and application requirements. Question:
We're looking to hire a carbon credit trader and want to claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus. What are the eligibility requirements for the candidate under the SOL for that role?
Expected:
For the carbon credit trader/broker role on the SOL, the candidate must minimally meet both criteria (1) and (2): (1) Minimum total three years' work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields. (2) At least a degree-equivalent qualification (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2. Qualifications criteria). Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. The supporting agency is EnterpriseSG. Note that the job duties for this role include trading or brokering carbon credits, originating and structuring carbon credit deals, facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits, and performing carbon market research and analysis on topics such as different types of carbon credits and their attributes, quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework), factors that affect price dynamics in over-the-counter markets or exchanges, and international regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6) as well as global and national compliance schemes and their evolving regulations (e.g. Australia, China, EU ETS). Additionally, the candidate needs to perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation, meet the additional requirements as stated in the SOL Employer Guide, and you need to select the shortage occupation in the EP application. The candidate should select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application form, which must be the same as the occupation for the job advertisement in MyCareersFuture (unless exempted from the FCF job advertising requirement). This SOL was released in November 2025 and applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.
Actual:
For a **carbon credit trader** to qualify for the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus under the Shortage Occupation List, the candidate must meet the following eligibility requirements:

## Job Duties
The candidate must perform these key duties:
- Trade or broker carbon credits
- Originate and structure carbon credit deals
- Facilitate transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits
- Perform carbon market research and analysis on:
  - Different types of carbon credits and their attributes (project type, region, co-benefits, crediting mechanism)
  - Quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework)
  - Price dynamics in over-the-counter markets or exchanges
  - International regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6), as well as global and national compliance schemes

## Minimum Requirements
The candidate **must** meet **both** of these criteria:

1. **Minimum three years' total work experience** in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields

2. **At least a degree-equivalent qualification** (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2 Qualifications criterion)

## Additional Assessment
Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these minimum criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business.

## Application Requirements
- Select "Carbon trader" as the job title in the EP application form
- This must match the occupation advertised on MyCareersFuture (unless you're exempt from FCF advertising requirements)

q-0011simple3My company is lowering the salary of one of our EP holders. How far in advance do we need to notify MOM, and what happens if the new salary doesn't meet the qualifying threshold?You need to notify MOM **at least 1 month in advance** before lowering the salary of an EP holder. **What happens if the new salary doesn't meet the qualifying threshold:** The EP holder must continue to qualify for the Employment Pass with the revised salary. If the new salary falls below the EP qualifying salary for their age and sector, they will no longer be eligible for the EP. In that case, you should: - Cancel the current Employment Pass, and - Apply for a new work pass based on their eligibility (for example, an S Pass if they meet those criteria) You can use the [Self-Assessment Tool (SAT)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-s-pass-self-assessment-tool) to check whether the EP holder will still meet the qualifying salary and COMPASS requirements with the reduced salary before you proceed.The chatbot's answer covers all key points: 1-month advance notification, requirement to continue qualifying for the pass, using SAT to check eligibility, and canceling/applying for new pass if not qualifying. It adds helpful context about age/sector considerations and COMPASS without introducing errors.
Question:
My company is lowering the salary of one of our EP holders. How far in advance do we need to notify MOM, and what happens if the new salary doesn't meet the qualifying threshold?
Expected:
As an employer, you must submit a request to MOM at least 1 month before the date you intend to lower the salary. The pass holder must continue to qualify for the work pass they are holding with the revised salary. You may use the Self-Assessment Tool (SAT) to find out if they are likely to meet the qualifying salary. If they do not qualify with the revised salary, you should cancel the work pass and apply for a new work pass based on the pass holder's eligibility.
Actual:
You need to notify MOM **at least 1 month in advance** before lowering the salary of an EP holder.

**What happens if the new salary doesn't meet the qualifying threshold:**

The EP holder must continue to qualify for the Employment Pass with the revised salary. If the new salary falls below the EP qualifying salary for their age and sector, they will no longer be eligible for the EP.

In that case, you should:
- Cancel the current Employment Pass, and
- Apply for a new work pass based on their eligibility (for example, an S Pass if they meet those criteria)

You can use the [Self-Assessment Tool (SAT)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-s-pass-self-assessment-tool) to check whether the EP holder will still meet the qualifying salary and COMPASS requirements with the reduced salary before you proceed.

q-0012simple3We're a high-growth startup incorporated in Singapore and we want to know if we can qualify for the SEP bonus under COMPASS. We've raised about USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a VC that isn't EDBI or SEEDS Capital. Does that count, or does the funding have to come from specific investors?Your USD 12 million in VC funding **can** qualify you for the SEP bonus, but it depends on which VC firm invested. For high-growth startups, the SEP bonus programme (number 8 in the list) requires: - Incorporation in Singapore - Minimum investment of **USD 10 million over the past 36 months** ✓ (you meet this) - Funding received from **EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms** The key question is whether your VC is on the list of "recognised investment firms." The guidance notes there's a separate list of recognised investment firms, but the specific names aren't in the material I have access to. **What you should do:** Contact **EnterpriseSG** at ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg to confirm whether your VC investor qualifies as a recognised investment firm. They administer this programme and can tell you definitively whether your funding source counts. If your VC is recognised, the SEP bonus would give you **10 points** toward the 40-point COMPASS threshold for Employment Pass applications, valid for up to 3 years. Can I help with anything else about COMPASS or Employment Pass applications?The chatbot accurately covers all key points: the USD 10 million threshold, the 36-month timeframe, the requirement for funding from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms, and provides the correct EnterpriseSG contact email for verification. The additional detail about 10 points is helpful context.
Question:
We're a high-growth startup incorporated in Singapore and we want to know if we can qualify for the SEP bonus under COMPASS. We've raised about USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a VC that isn't EDBI or SEEDS Capital. Does that count, or does the funding have to come from specific investors?
Expected:
High-growth startups incorporated in Singapore can qualify for the SEP bonus if they have a minimum investment amount of USD 10 million over the past 36 months. The funding must have been received from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms (there is a separate list of recognised investment firms maintained for this purpose). So your startup would meet the investment threshold (USD 12 million exceeds the USD 10 million minimum) and the 36-month timeframe, but the VC firm you received funding from must be on the list of recognised investment firms. If it is not EDBI or SEEDS Capital, it needs to be one of the other firms on that recognised list. This programme is supported by Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG), and you can contact them at ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg for queries, including whether your investor qualifies.
Actual:
Your USD 12 million in VC funding **can** qualify you for the SEP bonus, but it depends on which VC firm invested.

For high-growth startups, the SEP bonus programme (number 8 in the list) requires:

- Incorporation in Singapore
- Minimum investment of **USD 10 million over the past 36 months** ✓ (you meet this)
- Funding received from **EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms**

The key question is whether your VC is on the list of "recognised investment firms." The guidance notes there's a separate list of recognised investment firms, but the specific names aren't in the material I have access to.

**What you should do:**

Contact **EnterpriseSG** at ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg to confirm whether your VC investor qualifies as a recognised investment firm. They administer this programme and can tell you definitively whether your funding source counts.

If your VC is recognised, the SEP bonus would give you **10 points** toward the 40-point COMPASS threshold for Employment Pass applications, valid for up to 3 years.

Can I help with anything else about COMPASS or Employment Pass applications?

Question:
We're hiring a 30-year-old candidate for a role in our banking company. What fixed monthly salary does he need to score points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion, and is there a way to get the maximum 20 points?
Expected:
For the Banking & Other Financial Services Activities n.e.c. sector, a 30-year-old candidate needs a fixed monthly salary of at least $11,251 to score 10 points under C1 (this is the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries in that sector). To score the maximum 20 points, the candidate needs at least $17,401 (the 90th percentile). These benchmarks were released in August 2025 and are updated once a year to reflect the latest market conditions. The required salary increases progressively with age, from age 23 to age 45. For this sector, the range starts at $7,510 (10 points) / $11,151 (20 points) for candidates aged 23 or below, and rises to $19,266 (10 points) / $30,793 (20 points) for candidates aged 45 or above. Additionally, candidates who do not meet the EP qualifying salary will not be eligible for an EP regardless of COMPASS points scored under C1. EP candidates earning at least $22,500 in fixed monthly salary are exempted from COMPASS entirely.
Actual:

**Banking & Other Financial Services Activities:**
- **10 points** (65th percentile): **$11,251** fixed monthly salary
- **20 points** (90th percentile): **$17,401** fixed monthly salary

**Yes, you can get the maximum 20 points** by offering a fixed monthly salary of at least **$17,401**.

The C1 criterion awards points on a sliding scale:
- **0 points** if below the 65th percentile
- **10 points** at the 65th percentile ($11,251)
- **20 points** at the 90th percentile ($17,401)
- Points scale proportionally between these thresholds

Keep in mind that candidates earning **$22,500 or more** in fixed monthly salary are exempt from COMPASS entirely, though they still need to meet the EP qualifying salary for their age and sector.

q-0014simple3I have an EP holder working for us who does manual labour. Do I need to buy medical insurance or work injury compensation insurance for them? I'm confused about what's actually required for EP holders versus other pass types.For your EP holder doing manual labour, here's what you need to know: **Medical insurance:** Not required. Unlike Work Permit and S Pass holders, there is no mandatory medical insurance requirement for Employment Pass holders. **Work injury compensation insurance:** Yes, required. You must buy work injury compensation insurance for any EP holder who is a manual worker. The key distinction is that while EP holders generally don't need medical insurance coverage from employers, the work injury compensation insurance requirement is triggered specifically by the nature of the work—if they're doing manual labour, you must have that coverage. Even if you don't purchase work injury compensation insurance, you're still legally obligated to pay compensation for any eligible workplace injury claim under the Work Injury Compensation Act (WICA). Can I help with anything else regarding your EP holder's employment requirements?The chatbot accurately covers all key points: medical insurance not required for EP holders (unlike WP/S Pass), WIC insurance required for manual workers regardless of pass type, and WICA obligations apply even without insurance. The answer is clear, accurate, and substantively equivalent to the reference.
Question:
I have an EP holder working for us who does manual labour. Do I need to buy medical insurance or work injury compensation insurance for them? I'm confused about what's actually required for EP holders versus other pass types.
Expected:
Providing medical insurance is not a requirement for employing Employment Pass (EP) holders. Most EP holders may already have their own medical insurance or may want to choose their own coverage. For EP holders without existing medical insurance, employers can choose to provide insurance coverage as part of the employment contract, or encourage them to get their own. The medical insurance requirement applies only to Work Permit and S Pass holders, in line with employers' existing responsibility for the upkeep and maintenance of their migrant workers, including the provision of medical treatment.

However, for work injury compensation (WIC) insurance, buying it is only mandatory if you are employing manual workers or non-manual workers earning less than $1,600 a month. So if your EP holder is a manual worker, you are required to purchase work injury compensation insurance for them. Whether or not you have purchased WIC insurance, you are still required to pay compensation for an eligible claim.

Additionally, under the Work Injury Compensation Act (WICA), you are obligated to pay any eligible claims for workplace injuries. You are also obligated to provide paid sick leave, including dental leave, to any employee covered under the Employment Act.
Actual:
For your EP holder doing manual labour, here's what you need to know:

**Medical insurance:** Not required. Unlike Work Permit and S Pass holders, there is no mandatory medical insurance requirement for Employment Pass holders.

**Work injury compensation insurance:** Yes, required. You must buy work injury compensation insurance for any EP holder who is a manual worker.

The key distinction is that while EP holders generally don't need medical insurance coverage from employers, the work injury compensation insurance requirement is triggered specifically by the nature of the work—if they're doing manual labour, you must have that coverage.

Even if you don't purchase work injury compensation insurance, you're still legally obligated to pay compensation for any eligible workplace injury claim under the Work Injury Compensation Act (WICA).

Can I help with anything else regarding your EP holder's employment requirements?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately covers all key points: medical insurance not required for EP holders (unlike WP/S Pass), WIC insurance required for manual workers regardless of pass type, and WICA obligations apply even without insurance. The answer is clear, accurate, and substantively equivalent to the reference.

Question:
We have a candidate who's currently on a Dependant's Pass under an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder — not the spouse, just a dependent. They want to work for us. Can they just get a Letter of Consent like spouses do, or do they need a separate work pass?
Expected:
Only spouses of Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holders can work or operate a business in Singapore with a Letter of Consent (LOC). Other dependants who are not spouses will need to obtain a work pass to work for an employer in Singapore. If the dependant obtains an EP or S Pass, they will be granted the work and stay privileges associated with that pass, their stay will no longer depend on the main Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, and they will need to cancel their Dependant's Pass before the EP or S Pass is issued. If the dependant obtains a Work Permit instead, they must hold on to both the Work Permit and the Dependant's Pass; the Work Permit validity will be tied to the DP's validity; they can apply for any Work Permit regardless of nationality or citizenship; they are not subject to the six-monthly medical examination, security bond, or pregnancy restrictions; and they do not need medical insurance from the employer if they are already covered under a medical insurance plan that meets MOM's prevailing minimum mandatory insurance coverage requirement.
Actual:
They'll need a separate work pass. Only **spouses** of ONE Pass holders on a Dependant's Pass can work via a free Letter of Consent. Other dependants — such as children — must obtain a work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through an employer to work in Singapore.

Your candidate would need to apply for the appropriate work pass based on their qualifications and the role you're offering. If they qualify, you'd apply for an Employment Pass or S Pass on their behalf in the usual way.

Can I help with anything else about the application process?

Question:
We're looking to hire a carbon credit trader and want to claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus under the SOL. What are the requirements the candidate needs to meet for that specific role?
Expected:
For the Carbon credit trader role on the SOL, the candidate must minimally meet both of the following criteria: (1) Minimum total three years' work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields, and (2) At least a degree-equivalent qualification (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2. Qualifications criteria). Additionally, Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. The supporting agency is EnterpriseSG. Note that unlike many other SOL occupations where MOM verifies the firm's involvement in the relevant space directly with agencies, for this role it is EnterpriseSG that assesses the applications. The candidate also needs to perform the listed job duties, which include: trading or brokering carbon credits; originating and structuring carbon credit deals; facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits; and performing carbon market research and analysis on topics such as different types of carbon credits and their attributes, quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework), factors affecting price dynamics in OTC markets or exchanges, and international regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6) as well as global and national compliance schemes and their evolving regulations (e.g. Australia, China, EU ETS). You must also select the shortage occupation in the EP application, and the job title selected must match the occupation used for the job advertisement on MyCareersFuture (unless exempted from the FCF job advertising requirement). This SOL was released in November 2025 and applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.

The candidate must perform these key responsibilities:

- Facilitate transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits

q-0018simple2My partner holds an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, and I'm their common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass issued by MOM. I want to work in Singapore — what do I need to do, and how long will it take to get approved?As a common-law spouse of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you can work in Singapore by obtaining a **Letter of Consent (LOC)**. Your prospective employer needs to apply for the LOC on your behalf. Here's what they'll do: 1. Download and complete the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form 2. Submit the completed form with supporting documents online **Processing time: approximately 8 weeks** Before applying, make sure your LTVP has at least 3 months' validity remaining — if it doesn't, you'll need to renew it first. Question:
My partner holds an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, and I'm their common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass issued by MOM. I want to work in Singapore — what do I need to do, and how long will it take to get approved?
Expected:
As a common-law spouse holding a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) issued by MOM under an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, you need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC). Your prospective employer must complete the application on your behalf by: (1) downloading and filling in the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form, and (2) submitting the completed form with the relevant supporting documents online at go.gov.sg/mom-submit-loc-cls. The form should be completed using Adobe Acrobat Reader DC, as it may not appear correctly with other PDF readers. The form can only be used if the LTVP was issued by the Ministry of Manpower. If the LTVP is valid for less than 3 months, it must be renewed before submitting the application. The duration applied for can be up to 60 months, though if approved, the period granted may be shorter than what was indicated. It takes around 8 weeks to process the application. Supporting documents required include: the original completed and signed application form (1 PDF, up to 7 MB), the travel document page showing the applicant's personal particulars (1 PDF or JPG, up to 1 MB), and — if applicable — an SFA licence for food establishments (1 PDF, up to 2 MB), a support letter from Enterprise Singapore for Representative Office applications (1 PDF, up to 3 MB), and registration or support letters from the relevant vetting agency/professional body/accreditation agency if the applicant will take on regulated occupations such as Dentist (Singapore Dental Council), Doctor (Singapore Medical Council), Emergency Medical Technician or Paramedic (Unit for Prehospital Emergency Care), Lawyer (Legal Services Regulatory Authority), Diagnostic radiographer/Occupational therapist/Physiotherapist/Radiation therapist/Speech therapist (Allied Health Professionals Council), Nurse (Singapore Nursing Board), Pharmacist (Singapore Pharmacy Council), TCM practitioner (Traditional Chinese Medicine Practitioners Board), or Football player/Coach (Sport Singapore) (1 PDF, up to 3 MB). Non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation done by a translation service provider. MOM may also ask for additional documents during review. Note: Long-Term Visit Pass holders who are not common-law spouses cannot use this LOC process and must instead obtain a work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer, meeting all the criteria for the relevant pass.
Actual:
As a common-law spouse of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you can work in Singapore by obtaining a **Letter of Consent (LOC)**.

Your prospective employer needs to apply for the LOC on your behalf. Here's what they'll do:

1. Download and complete the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form
2. Submit the completed form with supporting documents online

**Processing time: approximately 8 weeks**

Before applying, make sure your LTVP has at least 3 months' validity remaining — if it doesn't, you'll need to renew it first.

The application requires details about your employment (occupation, salary, work location), your qualifications and work experience, and your employer's company information. If you're working in certain regulated occupations like healthcare or law, you'll also need registration or support letters from the relevant professional bodies.

Question:
We're bringing in an integrated circuit engineer for our semiconductor fab. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Physics. I want to make sure we meet the requirements for this role under COMPASS — does the candidate need to satisfy both the semiconductor firm verification and the degree requirement, or is one sufficient? And do we need to submit any proof to MOM that we're in the semiconductor space?
Expected:
The candidate must meet both criteria simultaneously — it is not sufficient to satisfy only one. First, the candidate's employer must be a firm involved in the Semiconductor space; MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so no additional documentation from the employer is required. However, new entrants to the Semiconductor space can reach out to EDB. Second, the candidate must hold at least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering or science faculty, which includes but is not limited to qualifications relating to those fields. A Bachelor's in Physics would fall under a science faculty and should satisfy the degree requirement. Since MOM verifies the firm's semiconductor involvement directly with EDB, the employer does not need to submit additional documentation for that criterion.
Actual:
Perfect! I found the information you need. Looking at the **Integrated Circuit Engineer** role on the Shortage Occupation List (SOL), here's what your candidate must meet:

## Both Requirements Are Mandatory

Your candidate must satisfy **BOTH** criteria (1) **AND** (2):

1. **Semiconductor firm verification** – Your company must be from a firm involved in the Semiconductor space. MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so **no additional documentation is required from you**. (If you're a new entrant to the semiconductor space, you can reach out to EDB.)

2. **Degree requirement** – The candidate must have at least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering/science faculty, including but not limited to:
   - Electrical Engineering
   - Mechanical Engineering  
   - Chemical Engineering
   - Materials Engineering
   - Material Sciences

A Bachelor's in **Physics** would qualify under the science faculty requirement.

## Key Points

- **Both conditions must be met** – it's not "either/or"
- **No proof needed for semiconductor status** – MOM verifies directly with EDB
- **Physics degree qualifies** – it falls under the science faculty category
- The supporting sector agency is **EDB**

Since your candidate has a Bachelor's in Physics and you're a semiconductor fab, you should meet both requirements for the Integrated Circuit Engineer SOL occupation.

Can I help with anything else regarding this EP application?

The chatbot accurately states both criteria must be met, explains MOM verifies semiconductor status with EDB (no employer documentation needed), notes new entrants can contact EDB, and correctly confirms a Physics degree qualifies under science faculty—all matching the reference answer.

q-0021medium3I'm applying for an Employment Pass for a cybersecurity engineer role. My candidate doesn't have a degree — he studied at a polytechnic — but he's been working as an ICT security architect for the past 4 years. Would he still qualify for the skills bonus under the shortage occupation list?Yes, your candidate can still qualify for the C5 Skills Bonus under the Shortage Occupation List for a cybersecurity engineer role, even without a degree. For the **Cybersecurity architect** occupation (which includes ICT security architect and ICT security engineer roles), the SOL provides two alternative pathways: **Either:** - **(1a)** At least a Bachelor's degree in Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies) **Or:** - **(1b)** Minimum total **three years' work experience** in one of the eligible job titles, including: - Cybersecurity architect - Cybersecurity engineer - **ICT security architect** ✓ - ICT security engineer - Information systems security developer Since your candidate has been working as an **ICT security architect for the past 4 years**, he meets the alternative pathway (1b) and qualifies for the C5 Skills Bonus — no degree required. When you apply, make sure to: - Select "ICT security architect" (or the matching title) as the job title in the EP application - Use the same occupation in your MyCareersFuture advertisement (unless you're exempt from advertising) - Include his 4 years of relevant work experience in the work experience section Can I help with anything else about the application?The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, correctly explaining both pathways (degree or 3 years experience), listing the eligible job titles, confirming the candidate qualifies via pathway 1b with 4 years as ICT security architect, and adding helpful practical application tips.
Question:

Since your candidate has 4 years of experience as an ICT security architect — which is one of the listed job titles and exceeds the 3-year minimum — he would meet the requirement under pathway (1b) even without a Bachelor's degree.

  - Cybersecurity architect

q-0022medium3We have a candidate's spouse who already has a Letter of Consent to work for another company. Now the spouse also started their own business here. Can we apply for a separate LOC so the spouse can work for us as well?No, a spouse cannot hold multiple Letters of Consent. A spouse can only hold **one LOC at a time**, as they are only allowed to work for one employer. If the spouse owns a business, they are considered employed by their own company and **cannot apply for an additional LOC to work for another employer**. Business ownership counts as employment. So in your situation, since the spouse already has an LOC to work for another company **and** has started their own business, they cannot obtain a separate LOC to work for your company as well. They would need to choose between: 1. Working for the current employer (keeping the existing LOC) 2. Running their own business (which counts as employment by their own company) 3. Working for your company (would need to cancel the existing LOC first) But they cannot do all three simultaneously under the current rules. Can I help with anything else regarding work pass matters?The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, covering all key points from the reference: only one LOC at a time, business ownership counts as employment, and therefore the spouse cannot get an additional LOC. It also adds helpful context about the options available.
Question:
We have a candidate's spouse who already has a Letter of Consent to work for another company. Now the spouse also started their own business here. Can we apply for a separate LOC so the spouse can work for us as well?
Expected:
No. A spouse can only hold one LOC at a time, as they are only allowed to work for one employer. Furthermore, if the spouse owns a business, they are considered employed by their own company, which means they cannot apply for an additional LOC to work for another employer. So in this case, the spouse who owns a business is already treated as employed by that business and would not be eligible for a second LOC to work for your company.
Actual:
No, a spouse cannot hold multiple Letters of Consent. A spouse can only hold **one LOC at a time**, as they are only allowed to work for one employer.

If the spouse owns a business, they are considered employed by their own company and **cannot apply for an additional LOC to work for another employer**. Business ownership counts as employment.

So in your situation, since the spouse already has an LOC to work for another company **and** has started their own business, they cannot obtain a separate LOC to work for your company as well. They would need to choose between:

1. Working for the current employer (keeping the existing LOC)
2. Running their own business (which counts as employment by their own company)
3. Working for your company (would need to cancel the existing LOC first)

But they cannot do all three simultaneously under the current rules.

Can I help with anything else regarding work pass matters?

Question:
We're putting together an EP application for a candidate who's 30 years old for our technology consultancy company. I want to understand the COMPASS salary benchmarks — what fixed monthly salary do we need to hit for the C1 criterion to score points, and what's the difference between the 10-point and 20-point thresholds at that age?

The difference between the two thresholds at age 30 is $4,988.
Actual:
For a 30-year-old candidate in a technology consultancy, the relevant sector is **Info-communication Technology (ICT)** or **Professional Services**, depending on your company's exact classification. Let me give you the benchmarks for both:

## Info-communication Technology sector

For a **30-year-old** candidate:
- **10 points (65th percentile)**: $8,971/month
- **20 points (90th percentile)**: $13,959/month

**Difference**: $4,988/month between the two thresholds

## Professional Services sector

For a **30-year-old** candidate:
- **10 points (65th percentile)**: $8,010/month  
- **20 points (90th percentile)**: $13,772/month

**Difference**: $5,762/month between the two thresholds

---

**Key points:**

1. The 10-point threshold gets your candidate into the COMPASS scoring system; the 20-point threshold maximizes the C1 salary score
2. These are the benchmarks currently in force (released August 2025)
3. Your candidate must meet at least the EP qualifying salary for their age first — for a 30-year-old that's $7,223/month in general sectors
4. If your candidate earns **$22,500 or more** in fixed monthly salary, they're exempt from COMPASS entirely

q-0029complex3We're looking to hire a foreign specialist for a role that's on the Shortage Occupation List. Our candidate graduated from KU Leuven in Belgium. When we submit the EP application, do we just pick any job title, or is there a specific process we need to follow for the SOL role — and will the candidate's university help with the application?For a Shortage Occupation List (SOL) role, there is a **specific process** you must follow when submitting the EP application: **1. Select the exact job title from the SOL** You must select one of the eligible job titles listed for that shortage occupation in the EP application form. This job title must match the occupation you advertised on MyCareersFuture (unless you're exempt from the Fair Consideration Framework advertising requirement). **2. Meet the additional requirements** Your candidate must meet the specific additional requirements for that SOL occupation, which can include: - **Relevant qualifications** – Upload these under the qualifications section of the EP application form - **Work experience in relevant job titles** – Select these under the work experience section - **Registration proof** (if applicable) – Upload this in the agency support question in the EP application form **3. Regarding your candidate's university** KU Leuven (Belgium) is on the **top-tier institutions list** for COMPASS C2 (Qualifications), which means: - Your candidate will earn **20 points** under COMPASS C2 for their degree from KU Leuven - The university itself doesn't "help with the application" – you still need to provide **verification proof** that the qualification is authentic when you submit the EP application - You'll need to select "Katholieke Universiteit Leuven (KU Leuven)" from the drop-down menu in the application form **Important:** If your candidate needs the SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS (reach 40 points total), MOM will check that they actually perform the job duties listed for that specific shortage occupation and meet all the additional requirements. The SOL Employer Guide details exactly what's required for each occupation. Is there anything specific about the SOL occupation or the application process you'd like me to clarify?The chatbot accurately explains the SOL application process: selecting an eligible job title (not any title), meeting additional requirements, and confirms KU Leuven is recognized. It correctly clarifies the university doesn't directly help with the application but the credential is recognized. Covers all key points from the reference with helpful additional detail.
Question:
We're looking to hire a foreign specialist for a role that's on the Shortage Occupation List. Our candidate graduated from KU Leuven in Belgium. When we submit the EP application, do we just pick any job title, or is there a specific process we need to follow for the SOL role — and will the candidate's university help with the application?
Expected:
For a Shortage Occupation List (SOL) role, the SOL bonus recognises jobs requiring highly specialised skills that are in shortage in the local workforce, determined through a robust evaluation process considering industry needs and local workforce development efforts. To qualify for this bonus: (1) the candidate must perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation; (2) you must select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application (not just any title); and (3) the candidate must meet checks on additional job requirements if they fall into certain categories. Regarding the candidate's university, Katholieke Universiteit Leuven (KU Leuven) in Belgium is on the list of recognised institutions, which can support the EP application (e.g., for meeting qualification-related criteria). So the university credential from KU Leuven is recognised, but you still need to follow the specific SOL application requirements including selecting the correct eligible job title and ensuring the candidate meets the relevant job duty and additional requirement checks.
Actual:
For a Shortage Occupation List (SOL) role, there is a **specific process** you must follow when submitting the EP application:

**1. Select the exact job title from the SOL**

You must select one of the eligible job titles listed for that shortage occupation in the EP application form. This job title must match the occupation you advertised on MyCareersFuture (unless you're exempt from the Fair Consideration Framework advertising requirement).

**2. Meet the additional requirements**

Your candidate must meet the specific additional requirements for that SOL occupation, which can include:

- **Relevant qualifications** – Upload these under the qualifications section of the EP application form
- **Work experience in relevant job titles** – Select these under the work experience section
- **Registration proof** (if applicable) – Upload this in the agency support question in the EP application form

**3. Regarding your candidate's university**

KU Leuven (Belgium) is on the **top-tier institutions list** for COMPASS C2 (Qualifications), which means:

- Your candidate will earn **20 points** under COMPASS C2 for their degree from KU Leuven
- The university itself doesn't "help with the application" – you still need to provide **verification proof** that the qualification is authentic when you submit the EP application
- You'll need to select "Katholieke Universiteit Leuven (KU Leuven)" from the drop-down menu in the application form

**Important:** If your candidate needs the SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS (reach 40 points total), MOM will check that they actually perform the job duties listed for that specific shortage occupation and meet all the additional requirements. The SOL Employer Guide details exactly what's required for each occupation.

Is there anything specific about the SOL occupation or the application process you'd like me to clarify?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately explains the SOL application process: selecting an eligible job title (not any title), meeting additional requirements, and confirms KU Leuven is recognized. It correctly clarifies the university doesn't directly help with the application but the credential is recognized. Covers all key points from the reference with helpful additional detail.

Question:
We're looking to bring in a candidate on an EP for a Software Development Manager role. He doesn't have a degree in computer science or any related IT field — he studied mechanical engineering — but he does have about four years of experience as a Software Development Manager. Separately, we're also considering hiring a Relationship Manager for our family office private banking unit (we're a licensed FI with MAS). That candidate has two years of experience as a Relationship Manager in ultra-high net worth. Would both of these candidates qualify for the Skills Bonus under the shortage occupation list?
Expected:
For the Software Development Manager role, the candidate can qualify for the C5 Skills Bonus on the Shortage Occupation List even without a relevant degree, because the additional requirements allow either (1a) at least a Bachelor's degree in Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies), OR (1b) a minimum total of three years' work experience in at least one of the eligible job titles (Application development manager, Head of applications development, Software and applications manager, or Software development manager). Since your candidate has four years of experience as a Software Development Manager, he satisfies criterion (1b) and would be eligible. The supporting agency is IMDA.

For the Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth / family office / philanthropy) role, the candidate would NOT qualify. The additional requirements for that role require the candidate to meet BOTH criteria: (1) the employer must be a Singapore licensed FI with private banking business (which your firm satisfies — MOM verifies this directly with MAS, no additional documentation needed), AND (2) the candidate must have a minimum total of three years' work experience as a Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth / family office / philanthropy). Your candidate has only two years of experience, which falls short of the three-year minimum. The supporting agency is MAS.

So only the Software Development Manager candidate would qualify; the Relationship Manager candidate would not.
Actual:
Based on the Shortage Occupation List requirements, here's the answer for both candidates:

**Software Development Manager (Mechanical Engineering degree, 4 years' experience):**

**Yes, this candidate qualifies** for the C5 Skills Bonus. The SOL allows two pathways for Software Development Manager roles:

- **(1a)** A Bachelor's degree in Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies), **OR**
- **(1b)** Minimum three years' work experience in the specific job title

Your candidate meets pathway (1b) with four years of experience as a Software Development Manager. The mechanical engineering degree doesn't disqualify them — the experience pathway is sufficient on its own.

---

**Relationship Manager (Private Banking, 2 years' experience in UHNW):**

**No, this candidate does not qualify**. The SOL requires **both** criteria:

1. Employer must be a Singapore licensed FI with private banking business (you meet this ✓)
2. **Minimum three years' work experience** in the Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth/family office/philanthropy) role

Your candidate has only two years of experience, falling one year short of the requirement. There is no alternative qualification pathway for this role — the three-year minimum is mandatory.

Can I help with anything else regarding these applications?

q-0032complex3We want to appoint one of our EP holders as a director on the board of a subsidiary company we own. After we get MOM's approval, what do we need to do next, and when the EP comes up for renewal, do we need to do anything about the directorship approval as well?After MOM approves the Letter of Consent (LOC), you need to **register the directorship with ACRA**. That's the final step to complete the appointment. Regarding renewal: the LOC is valid only until your EP holder's current pass expires. When you renew the EP, you'll need to **apply for a new LOC** after the EP renewal is successfully approved. The directorship approval doesn't automatically carry over—it's tied to the specific EP validity period. So in summary: - **Next step now**: Register the directorship with ACRA - **At EP renewal**: Apply for a fresh LOC once the renewed EP is approved Is there anything else you'd like to know about the directorship process?The chatbot's answer covers all essential points: registering with ACRA after LOC approval, LOC validity tied to EP expiry, and need to apply for new LOC upon EP renewal. Regarding renewal: the LOC is valid only up to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so when you successfully renew the EP, you must apply for a new LOC for the directorship to continue.

ONE Pass holders cannot work in certain media or religious roles without prior MOM approval. The restricted list includes 161 occupations — predominantly editors, journalists, producers, reporters, presenters, and religious workers (pastors, priests, monks, imams, rabbis, missionaries). If the new occupation is on the restricted list, you must submit a request via MOM's online form and wait for approval before starting work.
