q-0034complex3I'm a researcher considering applying for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. I know it covers multiple sectors, but I'm not sure how the eligibility assessment works — do I just need to meet some general criteria, or is it more specific than that? And what kind of employer-related financial documents would I need to prepare, and how recent do they have to be?The ONE Pass eligibility is quite specific, not just general criteria. You need to meet **one of four distinct pathways**, and each has its own requirements: ## The Four Eligibility Pathways **1. Current overseas employment** – You earned at least S$30,000 fixed monthly salary for 12 consecutive months from an established company (market cap ≥US$500M or annual revenue ≥US$200M) **2. Prospective Singapore employment** – You'll earn at least S$30,000 monthly from an established Singapore company (same financial thresholds) **3. Current/recent Singapore employment** – You earned at least S$30,000 monthly for 12 consecutive months in Singapore **4. Outstanding achievements** – You have exceptional accomplishments in sports, arts and culture, or academia and research (no salary requirement) The assessment is case-by-case, and MOM reviews holistically whether you meet the criteria for your chosen pathway. ## Employer Financial Documents For the **employer-related financial documents**, you need: **What to provide:** - Documents showing the company's **market capitalisation** of at least US$500 million, OR - Documents showing **annual revenue** of at least US$200 million **Where to get them:** - Stock exchange filings - Annual reports - Financial statements from regulatory authorities - Third-party financial databases **How recent:** Obtained **within the last 3 months** **If unavailable:** You may provide the company's **latest audited Profit & Loss statements** instead – but only from third-party auditors. Documents signed by the company's management won't be accepted. **Important note:** Combined amounts from the entire global office can be considered and will be assessed case-by-case. For researchers specifically, if you're applying under the **outstanding achievements** pathway for academia and research, you'd need your CV highlighting publications/patents from the past 5 years, plus a recommendation letter from a local institution like an Autonomous University or A*STAR – but no employer financial documents. Can I help clarify which pathway best fits your situation?The chatbot accurately explains that eligibility is specific (case-by-case, must meet criteria for chosen pathway), details the four pathways including academia/research, correctly states employer financial documents must be within 3 months, and covers market cap/revenue requirements. It goes beyond the reference with helpful additional details about document sources and the outstanding achievements pathway for researchers.
Question:
I'm a researcher considering applying for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. I know it covers multiple sectors, but I'm not sure how the eligibility assessment works — do I just need to meet some general criteria, or is it more specific than that? And what kind of employer-related financial documents would I need to prepare, and how recent do they have to be?
Expected:
The Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass covers top talent in all sectors, including academia and research, so you would be eligible to apply as a researcher. For eligibility, candidates are assessed on a case-by-case basis. You must choose the category that best fits you and meet all the criteria within that chosen category — not just some general criteria. Regarding employer-related financial documents, you need to provide the latest financial information of your employer, obtained within the last 3 months. This includes documents indicating the latest market capitalisation or annual company revenue. Additionally, if you have income sources other than your fixed monthly salary, you must provide documents supporting those other income sources. You will also need employment verification proof from a background screening company.
Actual:
The ONE Pass eligibility is quite specific, not just general criteria. You need to meet **one of four distinct pathways**, and each has its own requirements:

## The Four Eligibility Pathways

**1. Current overseas employment** – You earned at least S$30,000 fixed monthly salary for 12 consecutive months from an established company (market cap ≥US$500M or annual revenue ≥US$200M)

**2. Prospective Singapore employment** – You'll earn at least S$30,000 monthly from an established Singapore company (same financial thresholds)

**3. Current/recent Singapore employment** – You earned at least S$30,000 monthly for 12 consecutive months in Singapore

**4. Outstanding achievements** – You have exceptional accomplishments in sports, arts and culture, or academia and research (no salary requirement)

The assessment is case-by-case, and MOM reviews holistically whether you meet the criteria for your chosen pathway.

## Employer Financial Documents

For the **employer-related financial documents**, you need:

**What to provide:**
- Documents showing the company's **market capitalisation** of at least US$500 million, OR
- Documents showing **annual revenue** of at least US$200 million

**Where to get them:**
- Stock exchange filings
- Annual reports
- Financial statements from regulatory authorities
- Third-party financial databases

**How recent:** Obtained **within the last 3 months**

**If unavailable:** You may provide the company's **latest audited Profit & Loss statements** instead – but only from third-party auditors. Documents signed by the company's management won't be accepted.

**Important note:** Combined amounts from the entire global office can be considered and will be assessed case-by-case.

For researchers specifically, if you're applying under the **outstanding achievements** pathway for academia and research, you'd need your CV highlighting publications/patents from the past 5 years, plus a recommendation letter from a local institution like an Autonomous University or A*STAR – but no employer financial documents.

Can I help clarify which pathway best fits your situation?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately explains that eligibility is specific (case-by-case, must meet criteria for chosen pathway), details the four pathways including academia/research, correctly states employer financial documents must be within 3 months, and covers market cap/revenue requirements. It goes beyond the reference with helpful additional details about document sources and the outstanding achievements pathway for researchers.

q-0035complex3We have an ONE Pass holder whose common-law partner wants to work here in Singapore, and the pass holder also wants to bring over their 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, plus their 19-year-old stepchild. Can you walk me through what passes each of these family members would get, and whether any of them can work?Based on the ONE Pass holder's family situation, here's what each family member qualifies for: ## **Common-law partner:** - **Pass type:** Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) - **Can work:** Yes, through a Letter of Consent (LOC). The prospective employer applies for the LOC on their behalf, and processing takes around 8 weeks. The LTVP must have at least 3 months validity before applying for the LOC. ## **22-year-old unmarried son with disability:** - **Pass type:** Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) - **Can work:** No, not through the LTVP. He would need to obtain his own work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer if he wants to work. He'd need to meet all the criteria for whichever pass the employer applies for. ## **19-year-old stepchild:** - **Pass type:** Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) - **Can work:** No, not through the LTVP. Same as the 22-year-old—would need a separate work pass through an employer. **Key points:** - Stepchildren under 21 and handicapped children 21 and above both qualify for LTVP, not Dependant's Pass - Only the common-law spouse can get work rights directly through a Letter of Consent - The LOC application is free, but the LTVP itself takes 4 weeks to process - It's recommended to apply for family passes after the ONE Pass is approved to avoid losing application fees if the main pass is rejected Can I help with anything else about these passes?Correctly identifies all three family members as LTVP holders, correctly states the common-law partner can work via LOC, and correctly notes the handicapped son and stepchild cannot get LOC through their LTVP. Additional details about processing times and work pass alternatives are helpful extras, not errors.
Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose common-law partner wants to work here in Singapore, and the pass holder also wants to bring over their 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, plus their 19-year-old stepchild. Can you walk me through what passes each of these family members would get, and whether any of them can work?
Expected:
The common-law spouse (whose common-law relationship is recognised under the laws of their country or region) would be issued a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) and can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore. The 22-year-old unmarried handicapped son would also be eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass, as handicapped children who are unmarried and aged 21 and above qualify for an LTVP. The 19-year-old unmarried stepchild would likewise qualify for a Long-Term Visit Pass, since stepchildren who are unmarried and under 21 years old are eligible for an LTVP. Note that the LTVP for the handicapped son and the stepchild does not mention eligibility for a Letter of Consent to work — only spouses and common-law spouses on their respective passes are described as being able to apply for an LOC to work in Singapore.
Actual:
Based on the ONE Pass holder's family situation, here's what each family member qualifies for:

## **Common-law partner:**
- **Pass type:** Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)
- **Can work:** Yes, through a Letter of Consent (LOC). The prospective employer applies for the LOC on their behalf, and processing takes around 8 weeks. The LTVP must have at least 3 months validity before applying for the LOC.

## **22-year-old unmarried son with disability:**
- **Pass type:** Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)
- **Can work:** No, not through the LTVP. He would need to obtain his own work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer if he wants to work. He'd need to meet all the criteria for whichever pass the employer applies for.

## **19-year-old stepchild:**
- **Pass type:** Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)
- **Can work:** No, not through the LTVP. Same as the 22-year-old—would need a separate work pass through an employer.

**Key points:**
- Stepchildren under 21 and handicapped children 21 and above both qualify for LTVP, not Dependant's Pass
- Only the common-law spouse can get work rights directly through a Letter of Consent
- The LOC application is free, but the LTVP itself takes 4 weeks to process
- It's recommended to apply for family passes after the ONE Pass is approved to avoid losing application fees if the main pass is rejected

Can I help with anything else about these passes?

Correctly identifies all three family members as LTVP holders, correctly states the common-law partner can work via LOC, and correctly notes the handicapped son and stepchild cannot get LOC through their LTVP. q-0036complex3We're filing an LTVP application for a ONE Pass holder's spouse who wants to work in Singapore. The spouse has support from the Singapore Medical Council, and most of their documents — including the SMC support letter — are in Mandarin. Also, the spouse previously entered Singapore on a passport from a different country than the one they're using now. How should we handle all of this on the application?Based on the guidance, here's how to handle your LTVP Letter of Consent application: **Document language:** Non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation. This includes the SMC support letter and any other documents in Mandarin. The translation can be done by a translation service provider — you don't need a certified translator, but the documents must be translated. **SMC support:** On the application form (Part 4E), tick "Yes" when asked if the application has obtained support from the relevant vetting agency/professional body. Then select "Singapore Medical Council" from the list provided. You must submit the SMC support letter (with English translation) as documentary proof together with the application. **Previous passport from different country:** In Part 5 of the application form, there's a specific question: "Has the applicant ever entered Singapore using a passport issued by a different country?" Tick "Yes" and provide the details in the space provided at the bottom of that section. This is a standard disclosure requirement. **Submission:** Once the form is completed and signed by both the applicant (Part 6) and the employer (Part 7), submit it online at go.gov.sg/mom-submit-loc-cls along with: - The completed application form (PDF) - Travel document biodata page (PDF or JPG) - SMC support letter with English translation (PDF) - Any other required documents with translations Processing takes approximately 8 weeks. The LTVP must have at least 3 months' validity before you apply. Can I help with anything else about the application?The chatbot accurately addresses all three key points from the reference: English translation requirement for Mandarin documents, selecting SMC support in Part 4E with documentary proof, and declaring previous passport usage in Part 5. Additional practical details about submission are helpful, not misleading.
Question:
We're filing an LTVP application for a ONE Pass holder's spouse who wants to work in Singapore. The spouse has support from the Singapore Medical Council, and most of their documents — including the SMC support letter — are in Mandarin. Also, the spouse previously entered Singapore on a passport from a different country than the one they're using now. How should we handle all of this on the application?
Expected:
There are several things to address in this application: (1) Since the applicant has obtained support from the Singapore Medical Council, which is one of the listed Vetting Agencies on the form, you must select 'Yes' to indicate vetting agency support and select 'Singapore Medical Council' from the list. You must also produce documentary proof of support from the Singapore Medical Council together with the application. (2) All non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation, and the translation can be done by a translation service provider. So the Mandarin documents, including the SMC support letter if it is in Mandarin, need to have English translations attached. (3) In Part 5 – Other Information of the application form, the applicant must declare 'Yes' to the question asking whether they have entered Singapore using a passport issued by a different country, since that applies to their situation. Additionally, by submitting the application, the applicant consents to the Government of Singapore and statutory authorities obtaining from and verifying information with any person, organisation, or any other source.
Actual:
Based on the guidance, here's how to handle your LTVP Letter of Consent application:

**Document language:**
Non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation. This includes the SMC support letter and any other documents in Mandarin. The translation can be done by a translation service provider — you don't need a certified translator, but the documents must be translated.

**SMC support:**
On the application form (Part 4E), tick "Yes" when asked if the application has obtained support from the relevant vetting agency/professional body. Then select "Singapore Medical Council" from the list provided. You must submit the SMC support letter (with English translation) as documentary proof together with the application.

**Previous passport from different country:**
In Part 5 of the application form, there's a specific question: "Has the applicant ever entered Singapore using a passport issued by a different country?" Tick "Yes" and provide the details in the space provided at the bottom of that section. This is a standard disclosure requirement.

**Submission:**
Once the form is completed and signed by both the applicant (Part 6) and the employer (Part 7), submit it online at go.gov.sg/mom-submit-loc-cls along with:
- The completed application form (PDF)
- Travel document biodata page (PDF or JPG)
- SMC support letter with English translation (PDF)
- Any other required documents with translations

Processing takes approximately 8 weeks. The LTVP must have at least 3 months' validity before you apply.

Can I help with anything else about the application?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately addresses all three key points from the reference: English translation requirement for Mandarin documents, selecting SMC support in Part 4E with documentary proof, and declaring previous passport usage in Part 5. Additional practical details about submission are helpful, not misleading.

Question:
My company is terminating an EP holder and their last day is 15 February. We want to submit the cancellation early, and we also want to make sure the person can stay in Singapore for a while after the pass is cancelled to settle personal matters. Also, if MOM asks us for extra documents during the process, how do we send those in? And one more thing — after cancellation, if the person still has their physical EP card, what should we do with it?
Expected:
You can submit the cancellation request up to 14 days in advance — so for a 15 February cancellation, you can submit from 1 February onwards. The cancellation is immediate unless it is an advance cancellation request. The employer or employment agent can submit the cancellation. Upon cancellation (or within a day after the EP expiration date), you can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) for the pass holder, which grants valid stay in Singapore for up to 90 days. If MOM emails you requesting more documents, you can submit them using the online form at https://form.gov.sg/#!/5f8d4eadef8dab00112fd313. Regarding the physical EP card, if the card is found (or still in possession), it should be cut in half and discarded to prevent misuse.
Actual:
Perfect! I have all the information needed to answer your questions about EP cancellation. Here's what you need to know:

## Advance Cancellation

You can submit the cancellation request **up to 14 days in advance**. Since the last day is 15 February, you can submit the cancellation from 1 February onwards. The cancellation will take effect on 15 February as specified.

## Post-Cancellation Stay (STVP)

When you submit the cancellation, you can **request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP)** for the pass holder. The STVP grants valid stay in Singapore for **up to 90 days** from the date of application. This allows them time to settle personal matters.

You can request the STVP:
- At the time of cancellation, or
- Within one day after the EP expiration date

Both you and the pass holder will receive the STVP by email. They'll need to show it along with their valid passport when leaving Singapore.

## Submitting Extra Documents

If MOM requests additional documents during the process, you can submit them using this **[online form](https://form.gov.sg/#!/5f8d4eadef8dab00112fd313)**.

## Physical EP Card After Cancellation

Once the EP is cancelled, please **cut the card in half and discard it** to prevent misuse. The pass holder cannot work while on the STVP, even though they're still in Singapore.

**Important reminder:** Make sure you've completed the pre-cancellation steps including seeking tax clearance from IRAS at least 1 month before the last day of employment, and settling all outstanding employment issues.

q-0038complex3We have a candidate currently based overseas whom we're looking to bring in on a ONE Pass. If the main application gets rejected, what happens to the dependant pass applications we submitted at the same time — do we get the fees back? And once the candidate is here and the pass is issued, is there an annual reporting obligation they need to be aware of right away, or is there some grace period?Based on the guidance, here's what you need to know: **Dependant pass fees if the main application is rejected:** You will **not** get the fees back. If you submit family pass applications at the same time as the ONE Pass application and the main pass is rejected, the family pass applications will also be rejected and all fees paid are non-refundable. That's why MOM recommends applying for family passes only after the ONE Pass is approved — Dependant's Pass and Long-Term Visit Pass applications take 4 weeks to process anyway. **Annual notification obligation:** There is an annual reporting requirement, but it's **not immediate** when the pass is issued. ONE Pass holders who need to submit the annual notification will receive an email from MOM with instructions. Generally, pass holders who have just been issued their pass are excepted from the first round. The annual notification covers: - Employment details (employer(s) and occupation(s)) - Total annual income for the past calendar year - Other professional activities, if applicable The notification can be submitted by the pass holder themselves, their employer, or an appointed employment agency — only one submission is needed. Can I help with anything else about the ONE Pass or the application process?The chatbot accurately addresses both questions: fees are non-refundable if the main application is rejected, and newly issued pass holders are excepted from the immediate annual notification requirement. It also provides helpful additional context about the notification process.
Question:
We have a candidate currently based overseas whom we're looking to bring in on a ONE Pass. If the main application gets rejected, what happens to the dependant pass applications we submitted at the same time — do we get the fees back? And once the candidate is here and the pass is issued, is there an annual reporting obligation they need to be aware of right away, or is there some grace period?
Expected:
If the main ONE Pass holder's application is rejected, the family's dependant pass applications will also be rejected, and the fees paid for all of those applications are non-refundable. The candidate can apply for the pass while outside Singapore, but if approved they must make an appointment to get the pass issued at the Employment Pass Services Centre in Singapore. Once the pass is issued, ONE Pass holders are generally required to submit an annual notification, but there are exceptions — for example, those who have just been issued their pass are not required to submit the annual notification right away.
Actual:
Based on the guidance, here's what you need to know:

**Dependant pass fees if the main application is rejected:**

You will **not** get the fees back. If you submit family pass applications at the same time as the ONE Pass application and the main pass is rejected, the family pass applications will also be rejected and all fees paid are non-refundable. That's why MOM recommends applying for family passes only after the ONE Pass is approved — Dependant's Pass and Long-Term Visit Pass applications take 4 weeks to process anyway.

**Annual notification obligation:**

There is an annual reporting requirement, but it's **not immediate** when the pass is issued. ONE Pass holders who need to submit the annual notification will receive an email from MOM with instructions. Generally, pass holders who have just been issued their pass are excepted from the first round.

The annual notification covers:
- Employment details (employer(s) and occupation(s))
- Total annual income for the past calendar year
- Other professional activities, if applicable

The notification can be submitted by the pass holder themselves, their employer, or an appointed employment agency — only one submission is needed.

Can I help with anything else about the ONE Pass or the application process?

Question:
I'm going through the Employment Pass application process and I want to understand the full picture — if MOM asks me for additional documents, how do I submit them, and what kind of obligations or commitments does my employer (or I) take on as part of this application, especially regarding compliance and repatriation costs?
Expected:
If MOM requests additional documents during processing (which may take longer than 10 working days), you can submit them via the Employment Pass e-Service by clicking 'Work Passes' on the left menu, choosing the application record, and selecting 'Submit documents' (or selecting 'View details' to see the required action). Alternatively, MOM's email may direct you to submit documents using their online form. As part of the application, a declaration is made confirming that the applicant has read, understood, and will comply with all the Conditions of Employment Pass as specified in the Employment of Foreign Manpower (Work Passes) Regulations available on MOM's website, and will ensure these conditions are complied with. Additionally, there is an obligation to indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses which may be incurred by the Government in respect of the repatriation of the candidate or any of his/her dependants.
Actual:
Based on the Employment Pass application guidance, here's what you need to know:

## Submitting Additional Documents

If MOM requests additional documents during your application:

**Online submission via EP eService:**
- Log in to EP eService
- Click 'Work Passes' on the left menu
- Choose your application record
- Select 'Submit documents'
- Alternatively, select 'View details' to see what's required

**Alternative method:**
If MOM's email directs you to, use their [online form](https://form.gov.sg/#!/5f8d4eadef8dab00112fd313) to submit the requested documents.

**Important:** When additional documents are requested, your processing time may extend beyond the standard 10 working days.

---

## Employer Obligations & Commitments

Your employer takes on several key responsibilities when applying for your EP:

**Financial obligations:**
- **No requirement to demand kickbacks** – Your employer cannot demand or receive any sum or benefit from employment agencies or anyone else in connection with your employment
- **Salary payment** – Must pay your full fixed monthly salary within 7 days after each salary period (max 1 month)

**Repatriation costs:**
While the loaded content doesn't explicitly detail EP repatriation obligations (those are more extensively covered for Work Permit and S Pass holders), employers generally bear responsibility for employment-related costs.

**Compliance commitments:**
- Must not reduce your salary below what was declared in the application without MOM reassessment
- Must inform MOM if you breach any EP conditions
- Must cancel your EP within 7 days after your employment ends

**Your obligations as the EP holder:**
- Work only for the specified employer
- Update MOM of your residential address within 14 days of any change
- Comply with all EP conditions

Would you like me to clarify anything about the document submission process or the specific obligations?

q-0040complex3We're bringing in a senior hire on a ONE Pass, and the candidate wants to understand what they're committing to when they sign the application. Specifically, they're asking whether they can freely take up any role once the pass is issued, and whether there are any conduct-related undertakings they need to be aware of during their stay. Can you clarify what obligations they'd be agreeing to?Your candidate will sign a declaration form when the ONE Pass is issued. Here's what they're committing to: **Work flexibility – yes, with one restriction:** They can work for multiple companies concurrently and don't need to reapply when changing jobs. However, they must obtain MOM's prior approval before working in any **restricted occupation** – predominantly media roles (editors, journalists, producers, reporters) and religious roles (pastors, priests, monks, imams, rabbis, missionaries). The full list has 161 occupations. For any other role, they're free to start without approval. **Key conduct and compliance undertakings:** - **Health declaration:** They confirm they don't have or haven't suffered from AIDS/HIV or tuberculosis. - **Self-sufficiency:** They're responsible for their own stay, maintenance, and repatriation, and will indemnify the Singapore Government for any related costs. - **Lawful conduct:** They undertake not to misuse controlled drugs or engage in political or other activities that would make them an undesirable or prohibited immigrant. - **Annual reporting:** They must report their professional activities to MOM at the end of each calendar year in the format and by the deadline MOM specifies. - **Address updates:** They must notify MOM within 14 days of any change to their contact details or residential address. - **Information consent:** They consent to MOM verifying information (including medical records and income tax records) with any person or organisation, and to sharing their details with government agencies for work-pass and emergency purposes. The declaration also warns that providing false or misleading information can result in prosecution, cancellation of approval, or revocation of the pass. Can I help with anything else about the ONE Pass?Covers all key points from the reference (restricted occupations requiring prior approval, conduct undertakings about drugs and political activities) and adds accurate supplementary details about other declaration requirements without contradicting the reference.
Question:
We're bringing in a senior hire on a ONE Pass, and the candidate wants to understand what they're committing to when they sign the application. Specifically, they're asking whether they can freely take up any role once the pass is issued, and whether there are any conduct-related undertakings they need to be aware of during their stay. Can you clarify what obligations they'd be agreeing to?
Expected:
When signing the ONE Pass application, the candidate makes several undertakings. First, they declare that all information provided in the application and all supporting documents submitted are true and correct, and that the documents are true copies of the originals. Second, regarding conduct during their stay in Singapore, they undertake not to misuse controlled drugs and not to take part in any political or other activities that would make them an undesirable or prohibited immigrant under the Immigration Act. Third, regarding employment, they cannot freely take up any role — they must obtain prior approval from the Ministry of Manpower if they intend to start working in a restricted occupation specified by the Ministry after the ONE Pass is issued to them.
Actual:
Your candidate will sign a declaration form when the ONE Pass is issued. Here's what they're committing to:

**Work flexibility – yes, with one restriction:**

They can work for multiple companies concurrently and don't need to reapply when changing jobs. However, they must obtain MOM's prior approval before working in any **restricted occupation** – predominantly media roles (editors, journalists, producers, reporters) and religious roles (pastors, priests, monks, imams, rabbis, missionaries). The full list has 161 occupations. For any other role, they're free to start without approval.

**Key conduct and compliance undertakings:**

- **Health declaration:** They confirm they don't have or haven't suffered from AIDS/HIV or tuberculosis.

- **Self-sufficiency:** They're responsible for their own stay, maintenance, and repatriation, and will indemnify the Singapore Government for any related costs.

- **Lawful conduct:** They undertake not to misuse controlled drugs or engage in political or other activities that would make them an undesirable or prohibited immigrant.

- **Annual reporting:** They must report their professional activities to MOM at the end of each calendar year in the format and by the deadline MOM specifies.

- **Address updates:** They must notify MOM within 14 days of any change to their contact details or residential address.

- **Information consent:** They consent to MOM verifying information (including medical records and income tax records) with any person or organisation, and to sharing their details with government agencies for work-pass and emergency purposes.

The declaration also warns that providing false or misleading information can result in prosecution, cancellation of approval, or revocation of the pass.

Question:
We're submitting an EP application and need to verify the candidate's degree from NTU through their online verification portal. I want to make sure we get the screenshots right and also handle the MOM verification reference number properly. What exactly do we need to capture and upload for the educational qualification verification, and how does the MOM reference number fit into the process?
Expected:
For verifying the candidate's educational qualification through a university verification portal (e.g., NTU), you need to capture and upload two screenshots: (1) A 'Verification page' screenshot that must include the visible website address of the verification portal and all information entered in the portal, such as the candidate's name and date of birth. (2) A 'Verified page' screenshot that must include the visible website address of the verification portal and the final page showing that the candidate was awarded the educational qualification. Both the verification page and verified page screenshots should be uploaded. Separately, when dealing with the MOM verification reference number, you need to key in the 'MOM verification reference number' as stated on the verification proof or as provided by the background screening company, and then upload the verification proof from the background screening company. Additionally, when uploading any documents such as passport pages, you must ensure the whole page, including the entire 'Machine Readable Zone', is visible — it must not be cut off, blocked, or covered by a watermark.

q-0042complex3I'm on an Employment Pass under the Sponsorship scheme and my pass still has about 4 months left. I want to renew early — will I lose those remaining months? Also, what form do I need to use, and what kind of obligations or commitments does the renewal involve regarding repatriation?You won't lose those 4 months. When you renew early, any remaining validity from your current pass — up to six months — is added on to your renewal. So your renewed pass will start when your current pass expires, and you'll keep the full duration. For EP (Sponsorship), you need to use the **EP (Sponsorship) renewal application form**. You download it, complete it as a soft copy, and submit it along with supporting documents through the online form. You can start renewing 3 months before your pass expires. Regarding repatriation obligations, the renewal form includes a declaration and undertaking by your local sponsor. The sponsor commits to: - Be responsible for your stay, maintenance, and **repatriation** - Indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses incurred in respect of your repatriation or that of any of your dependants The sponsor also undertakes to cancel your Employment Pass within 7 days upon termination of employment. The renewed pass is valid for up to 3 years, and processing takes around 6 weeks for most EP (Sponsorship) cases. Can I help with anything else about your renewal?Covers all key points: remaining months carry over (up to 6 months), correct form (EP Sponsorship renewal form), and repatriation indemnity obligation. Question:
I'm on an Employment Pass under the Sponsorship scheme and my pass still has about 4 months left. I want to renew early — will I lose those remaining months? Also, what form do I need to use, and what kind of obligations or commitments does the renewal involve regarding repatriation?

If you renew your Employment Pass early, your new work pass validity will not be reduced or lost. Any remaining validity from your current pass — up to six months — is added on to your renewal, so your 4 remaining months would carry over. However, if you miss the renewal deadline, you would need to apply for a new pass instead. As an EP (Sponsorship) holder, you must use the specific EP Sponsorship renewal form for your renewal application. The form requires the candidate's name as shown on their travel document, their signature, and the date in DD/MM/YYYY format. As part of the renewal, there is an obligation to indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses that may be incurred by the Government in respect of the repatriation of the candidate or any of his/her dependants.
Actual:
You won't lose those 4 months. When you renew early, any remaining validity from your current pass — up to six months — is added on to your renewal. So your renewed pass will start when your current pass expires, and you'll keep the full duration.

For EP (Sponsorship), you need to use the **EP (Sponsorship) renewal application form**. You download it, complete it as a soft copy, and submit it along with supporting documents through the online form. You can start renewing 3 months before your pass expires.

- Be responsible for your stay, maintenance, and **repatriation**
- Indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses incurred in respect of your repatriation or that of any of your dependants

The sponsor also undertakes to cancel your Employment Pass within 7 days upon termination of employment.

q-0043complex2We have an ONE Pass holder whose spouse holds a Dependant's Pass and now wants to start her own business in Singapore. The DP currently has about 2 months of validity left. Can we go ahead and submit the Letter of Consent application for her, and who should be the one submitting it — us as the employer, or can she do it herself?No, you cannot submit the Letter of Consent application yet. The Dependant's Pass must have **at least 3 months' validity** before you can apply for the LOC. With only 2 months left, you need to renew the DP first. **Who submits the application:** Once the DP has sufficient validity, it depends on whether she's working for an employer or running her own business: - **For operating a business**: The candidate herself or an appointed employment agent can apply - **For working for an employer**: The employer must apply on behalf of the candidate Since she wants to start her own business, either she can submit the application herself or you can appoint an employment agent to do it. An employer is not the applicant in this scenario. **Key details:** - The LOC is free of charge - Processing takes around 8 weeks for most cases - The LOC will be valid up to the expiry date of the Dependant's Pass Renew the DP first to get it beyond 3 months' validity, then proceed with the LOC application.Covers all key points correctly (3-month validity requirement, who submits for business vs employment, LOC is free, validity tied to DP expiry), but states processing time as 8 weeks instead of the reference's 4 weeks, which is a minor inaccuracy.
Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose spouse holds a Dependant's Pass and now wants to start her own business in Singapore. The DP currently has about 2 months of validity left. Can we go ahead and submit the Letter of Consent application for her, and who should be the one submitting it — us as the employer, or can she do it herself?
Expected:
No, you cannot proceed with the application immediately. The Dependant's Pass must have at least 3 months of validity remaining, so since it only has about 2 months left, the DP needs to be renewed first before submitting the Letter of Consent (LOC) application. As for who submits it: because the spouse wishes to operate a business in Singapore (rather than work for an employer), the candidate herself or an appointed employment agent can apply — the employer does not need to submit it on her behalf. If she instead wanted to work for an employer in Singapore, then the employer would need to apply on her behalf. The LOC is free of charge, processing takes within 4 weeks for most cases, and its duration is up to the expiry date of the Dependant's Pass.
Actual:
No, you cannot submit the Letter of Consent application yet. The Dependant's Pass must have **at least 3 months' validity** before you can apply for the LOC. With only 2 months left, you need to renew the DP first.

**Who submits the application:**

Once the DP has sufficient validity, it depends on whether she's working for an employer or running her own business:

- **For operating a business**: The candidate herself or an appointed employment agent can apply
- **For working for an employer**: The employer must apply on behalf of the candidate

Since she wants to start her own business, either she can submit the application herself or you can appoint an employment agent to do it. An employer is not the applicant in this scenario.

**Key details:**
- The LOC is free of charge
- Processing takes around 8 weeks for most cases
- The LOC will be valid up to the expiry date of the Dependant's Pass

Renew the DP first to get it beyond 3 months' validity, then proceed with the LOC application.

The appeal must be submitted by your employer within 3 months of the rejection. **Regarding your found ONE Pass card:** Since you found your lost card, you must **return it to MOM immediately by post** to: Ministry of Manpower 18 Havelock Road Singapore 059764 This applies even if you've already applied for a replacement card. The guidance specifically states that if a lost card is found, it should be returned to prevent misuse. If you haven't yet applied for a replacement, you don't need to — you can simply continue using your original card now that you've found it. The replacement requirement only applies if the card remains lost, stolen, or damaged. q-0045hard-13We're bringing in a foreign candidate on an EP, and as part of his role he'll also need to sit on the board of one of our subsidiary companies that we hold shares in. I want to make sure I understand what's needed — does he need a separate work pass for that directorship, and when I'm submitting his EP application, how should I handle the educational qualification entry if his university isn't showing up in the dropdown list?Perfect — I have the information you need on both points. ## Secondary directorship Your EP holder **does not need a separate work pass** for the directorship. However, you do need to apply for a **Letter of Consent (LOC)** from MOM before appointing him to the subsidiary's board. MOM will grant the LOC if your subsidiary is related by corporate shareholding to his primary employer. This includes indirect shareholding relationships — for example, if both companies are subsidiaries of the same holding company, or if the subsidiary is a direct or indirect subsidiary of his primary employer. **Steps you need to take:** 1. Confirm the primary employer has no objection to the directorship 2. Apply for the LOC (processed within 5 weeks) 3. Once approved, register the directorship with ACRA The LOC is valid until the EP expires or is cancelled, and no payment for the directorship role is involved. ## Educational qualification entry If the university isn't in the dropdown list, **enter the institution's most recent name** where applicable. Alternatively, **you may choose not to declare the qualification** if you can't find it on the list. Declaring a qualification is only mandatory if your candidate needs points from the C2 Qualifications criterion to pass COMPASS, or if they're applying for a Shortage Occupation List role that requires qualifications to be declared. If you do declare it, you'll need verification proof — certificates and school letters are not acceptable. You can verify through a background screening company, the government/awarding institution's online portal, or OpenCerts for digital certificates. Can I help with anything else on the EP application?The chatbot correctly explains that an LOC is required (not a separate work pass), that MOM generally grants it for related companies, and provides accurate guidance on the educational qualification entry including entering the most recent name or choosing not to declare. The additional details about verification and COMPASS are helpful context, not noise.
Question:
We're bringing in a foreign candidate on an EP, and as part of his role he'll also need to sit on the board of one of our subsidiary companies that we hold shares in. I want to make sure I understand what's needed — does he need a separate work pass for that directorship, and when I'm submitting his EP application, how should I handle the educational qualification entry if his university isn't showing up in the dropdown list?
Expected:
For the secondary directorship: EP holders are generally only allowed to work for their employer as specified on their work pass. Taking up a directorship appointment (executive or non-executive) in another company constitutes a form of work under the Companies Act, so a work pass is required. The EP holder must apply for and be granted a Letter of Consent (LOC) under regulation 11 of the Employment of Foreign Manpower (Work Passes) Regulations 2012. MOM will generally grant an LOC to allow EP holders to take up secondary directorship appointments in companies that are related by corporate shareholding to the EP holder's primary employer, as these appointments may be critical to their primary job role. Since your subsidiary is related by corporate shareholding, MOM would generally grant the LOC.

For the educational qualification in the EP application: If the institution name on the educational certificate or verification proof is not available in the dropdown menu, you should enter the institution's most recent name where applicable. Alternatively, you may choose not to declare the qualification if you are unable to find it on the list. You should also enter the qualification level as per the verification proof or educational certificate, and enter the faculty as per the transcript if it is not found in the verification proof or educational certificate.
Actual:
Perfect — I have the information you need on both points.

## Secondary directorship

Your EP holder **does not need a separate work pass** for the directorship. However, you do need to apply for a **Letter of Consent (LOC)** from MOM before appointing him to the subsidiary's board.

MOM will grant the LOC if your subsidiary is related by corporate shareholding to his primary employer. This includes indirect shareholding relationships — for example, if both companies are subsidiaries of the same holding company, or if the subsidiary is a direct or indirect subsidiary of his primary employer.

**Steps you need to take:**

1. Confirm the primary employer has no objection to the directorship
2. Apply for the LOC (processed within 5 weeks)
3. Once approved, register the directorship with ACRA

The LOC is valid until the EP expires or is cancelled, and no payment for the directorship role is involved.

## Educational qualification entry

If the university isn't in the dropdown list, **enter the institution's most recent name** where applicable. Alternatively, **you may choose not to declare the qualification** if you can't find it on the list.

Declaring a qualification is only mandatory if your candidate needs points from the C2 Qualifications criterion to pass COMPASS, or if they're applying for a Shortage Occupation List role that requires qualifications to be declared.

If you do declare it, you'll need verification proof — certificates and school letters are not acceptable. You can verify through a background screening company, the government/awarding institution's online portal, or OpenCerts for digital certificates.

Can I help with anything else on the EP application?

q-0046hard-11I currently hold an Employment Pass but I'm thinking of switching to the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. If my ONE Pass application gets rejected, will MOM just convert it into an EP application automatically? Also, if I do get the ONE Pass, how do I go about cancelling my old pass — can I do it myself, and how long does the cancellation take?No, MOM will not automatically convert a rejected ONE Pass application into an Employment Pass application. If your ONE Pass application is unsuccessful, your employer will need to submit a separate EP application if you meet the EP eligibility requirements. Regarding cancelling your current EP: **You should not cancel your EP before applying.** You can apply for the ONE Pass while still holding your EP. Only cancel your EP **after** your ONE Pass application is approved and when you're ready to get the new pass issued. Once your EP is cancelled, you cannot work until your ONE Pass is issued. **Your employer cancels the EP, not you.** The cancellation itself is immediate once submitted. However, you'll want to time it carefully — get your ONE Pass approved first, then have your employer cancel the EP just before you go to get your ONE Pass issued. One important note: when your EP is cancelled, any family passes tied to it (like Dependant's Passes) will also be automatically cancelled. You'll need to apply for new family passes under your ONE Pass after it's approved — these take about 4 weeks to process. Can I help with anything else about the ONE Pass or the transition process?Correctly states no automatic conversion and that cancellation is immediate, but incorrectly claims only the employer can cancel the EP (reference says the pass holder or employment agent can do it) and omits the 1-week timing guidance for cancellation.
Question:
I currently hold an Employment Pass but I'm thinking of switching to the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. If my ONE Pass application gets rejected, will MOM just convert it into an EP application automatically? Also, if I do get the ONE Pass, how do I go about cancelling my old pass — can I do it myself, and how long does the cancellation take?
Expected:
No, you will not be automatically assessed for an Employment Pass if your Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass (ONE Pass) application is unsuccessful. Your employer should submit a separate application for the Employment Pass if you meet its eligibility requirements. Regarding cancellation of the pass (e.g., if you no longer plan to work in Singapore): you should cancel within 1 week before your departure date (for example, if your departure date is 10 February, submit the request from 3 February onwards). Either you or your employment agent can cancel the pass, and the cancellation is immediate.
Actual:
No, MOM will not automatically convert a rejected ONE Pass application into an Employment Pass application. If your ONE Pass application is unsuccessful, your employer will need to submit a separate EP application if you meet the EP eligibility requirements.

Regarding cancelling your current EP:

**You should not cancel your EP before applying.** You can apply for the ONE Pass while still holding your EP. Only cancel your EP **after** your ONE Pass application is approved and when you're ready to get the new pass issued. Once your EP is cancelled, you cannot work until your ONE Pass is issued.

**Your employer cancels the EP, not you.** The cancellation itself is immediate once submitted. However, you'll want to time it carefully — get your ONE Pass approved first, then have your employer cancel the EP just before you go to get your ONE Pass issued.

One important note: when your EP is cancelled, any family passes tied to it (like Dependant's Passes) will also be automatically cancelled. You'll need to apply for new family passes under your ONE Pass after it's approved — these take about 4 weeks to process.

Can I help with anything else about the ONE Pass or the transition process?

q-0047hard-13We're bringing in a candidate on the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. He has a 22-year-old unmarried son who he'd like to bring along to Singapore. Can we get a dependent pass for the son, and separately, once the candidate's own ONE Pass is approved in-principle, what payment methods are accepted when we go down to have the pass issued, and are there any ongoing obligations around updating MOM on address changes after issuance?Unfortunately, **a 22-year-old son is not eligible for a dependent pass**. For ONE Pass holders, only **unmarried children under 21** qualify for a Dependant's Pass. Since your candidate's son is 22, he falls outside this age limit and passes for family are not available to him. He would need to obtain his own work pass if he wishes to work in Singapore. Regarding pass issuance, once the ONE Pass is approved in-principle: **Payment methods** depend on how the pass is issued: - **Via EP eService** (with Singpass): GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex - **At the Employment Pass Services Centre** (without Singpass): Visa, Mastercard, Amex, NETS, or CashCard (ensure overseas-issued cards are activated for overseas use) The issuance fee is **$225**. **Address update obligations**: The pass holder must **update MOM within 2 weeks** of any change to their residential address or mobile number. This is done through EP eService, and the update is immediate. The pass holder should print the acknowledgement page and keep it with their pass card. q-0048hard-13My partner and I are in a common-law relationship that's recognised in our home country, and I'm on a Long-Term Visit Pass tied to an ONE Pass holder. I want to work in Singapore — do I need to get a separate work pass like an Employment Pass, or is there another way? And if I apply through my partner's employer, how long can the pass duration be?Good news — as a common-law spouse of an ONE Pass holder on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you can work in Singapore through a **Letter of Consent (LOC)**, not a separate work pass. Your prospective employer applies for the LOC on your behalf. The application takes around **8 weeks** to process, and your LTVP must have at least 3 months' validity before applying. **Pass duration:** If approved, the LOC can be granted for **up to 60 months** (5 years), though the actual period granted may be shorter. The LOC is tied to one employer at a time — if you own a business, that counts as employment by your own company, so you cannot hold an additional LOC to work for another employer. Other LTVP holders who aren't common-law spouses would need a separate work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer instead. Can I help with anything else about the LOC application process?The chatbot correctly explains that a Letter of Consent (LOC) is available for common-law spouses on LTVP tied to ONE Pass holders, that no separate work pass is needed, that the duration can be up to 60 months but may be shorter, and that other LTVP holders would need a work pass. All key points from the reference are covered accurately.
Question:
My partner and I are in a common-law relationship that's recognised in our home country, and I'm on a Long-Term Visit Pass tied to an ONE Pass holder. I want to work in Singapore — do I need to get a separate work pass like an Employment Pass, or is there another way? And if I apply through my partner's employer, how long can the pass duration be?
Expected:
As a common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) linked to an Overseas Networks & Expertise (ONE) Pass holder, you can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore. You do not need to obtain a separate work pass such as an Employment Pass. This is specifically for common-law spouses — other LTVP holders who are not common-law spouses would need to obtain a work pass to work in Singapore. When applying for the LOC, the pass duration applied for can be up to 60 months, although if the application is approved, the period granted may be shorter than what is indicated in the application.
Actual:
Good news — as a common-law spouse of an ONE Pass holder on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you can work in Singapore through a **Letter of Consent (LOC)**, not a separate work pass.

Other LTVP holders who aren't common-law spouses would need a separate work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer instead.

q-0049hard-13We're preparing an EP application for a candidate whose university doesn't appear in MOM's list of recognized institutions. On top of that, the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among our PMET staff, and I'm also wondering how our local PMET hiring ratio factors in. Can you walk me through what we need to handle for the qualification verification and how these workforce composition factors play into the application outcome?You need qualification verification proof when the university isn't on MOM's list. You have three options: **1. Background screening company** Engage an approved firm to verify the degree. They'll provide an MOM verification reference number that you enter in the EP application. If verification is still pending after 14 working days and the institution is from the drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit the receipt for the verification service instead. Once the pass is approved, you can bring the candidate in and issue the pass without submitting the final proof—but if the qualification turns out to be false, the pass will be revoked. **2. Government or awarding institution's online portal** If the university offers online verification, take screenshots showing the portal URL, candidate's name and qualification details, date of verification, and any reference number. Upload both the verification page and the verified result page. **3. OpenCerts digital certificate** If the institution issues blockchain-verified certificates via OpenCerts, upload that. Certificates and school letters are not acceptable verification proof. The verification must confirm the degree is authentic **and** that the institution is accredited (recognised by the local government). ## How workforce composition affects COMPASS **C3 – Diversity (up to 20 points)** This measures the candidate's nationality share among your firm's PMETs. If your firm has fewer than 25 PMETs, you score 10 points by default. Otherwise: - Nationality share <5%: 20 points - 5% to <25%: 10 points - ≥25%: 0 points **C4 – Support for Local Employment (up to 20 points)** This compares your local PMET share to your sector benchmark. If your firm has fewer than 25 PMETs, you score 10 points by default. Otherwise: - ≥50th percentile of sector: 20 points - 20th to <50th percentile: 10 points - <20th percentile: 0 points If your local PMET share is at least 70%, you score at least 10 points regardless of sector position. Use the Workforce Insights tool on myMOM Portal to check your firm's C3 and C4 scores—they're based on a rolling three-month average, so strengthening your workforce takes time. You need 40 points total across all six COMPASS criteria to pass (unless the candidate earns ≥$22,500/month fixed salary, which exempts them from COMPASS entirely). Can I help with anything else on the application?The chatbot provides comprehensive and accurate information covering both qualification verification (with detailed options beyond the reference) and workforce composition factors (C3 Diversity and C4 Local Employment) with specific point thresholds. It correctly explains that well-represented nationality yields fewer points and how local PMET share compares to sector benchmarks, fully addressing the user's question.
Question:
We're preparing an EP application for a candidate whose university doesn't appear in MOM's list of recognized institutions. On top of that, the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among our PMET staff, and I'm also wondering how our local PMET hiring ratio factors in. Can you walk me through what we need to handle for the qualification verification and how these workforce composition factors play into the application outcome?
Expected:
For the qualification verification: if the candidate's awarding institution cannot be found on MOM's list, you must enter its name exactly as it appears on the educational certificate, and the employer must upload a verification proof of the qualification to confirm it is genuine and from an accredited institution.

Regarding workforce composition factors under the COMPASS framework, two distinct criteria are relevant:

1. Nationality diversity criterion: This criterion awards more points to applications where the candidate's nationality forms a small share of the firm's PMET employees. A diverse mix of nationalities enriches firms with new ideas and networks and contributes to a more inclusive and resilient workforce. Since the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among your PMET staff, this criterion would award fewer points for the application.

2. Local PMET share criterion: Points are also affected by whether your firm has a relatively higher share of locals among PMET employees compared to your peers in the same sector. A higher local share relative to sector peers would be favorable.
Actual:
You need qualification verification proof when the university isn't on MOM's list. You have three options:

**1. Background screening company**  
Engage an approved firm to verify the degree. They'll provide an MOM verification reference number that you enter in the EP application. If verification is still pending after 14 working days and the institution is from the drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit the receipt for the verification service instead. Once the pass is approved, you can bring the candidate in and issue the pass without submitting the final proof—but if the qualification turns out to be false, the pass will be revoked.

**2. Government or awarding institution's online portal**  
If the university offers online verification, take screenshots showing the portal URL, candidate's name and qualification details, date of verification, and any reference number. Upload both the verification page and the verified result page.

**3. OpenCerts digital certificate**  
If the institution issues blockchain-verified certificates via OpenCerts, upload that.

Certificates and school letters are not acceptable verification proof. The verification must confirm the degree is authentic **and** that the institution is accredited (recognised by the local government).

## How workforce composition affects COMPASS

**C3 – Diversity (up to 20 points)**  
This measures the candidate's nationality share among your firm's PMETs. If your firm has fewer than 25 PMETs, you score 10 points by default. Otherwise:
- Nationality share <5%: 20 points  
- 5% to <25%: 10 points  
- ≥25%: 0 points

**C4 – Support for Local Employment (up to 20 points)**  
This compares your local PMET share to your sector benchmark. If your firm has fewer than 25 PMETs, you score 10 points by default. Otherwise:
- ≥50th percentile of sector: 20 points  
- 20th to <50th percentile: 10 points  
- <20th percentile: 0 points

If your local PMET share is at least 70%, you score at least 10 points regardless of sector position.

Use the Workforce Insights tool on myMOM Portal to check your firm's C3 and C4 scores—they're based on a rolling three-month average, so strengthening your workforce takes time. You need 40 points total across all six COMPASS criteria to pass (unless the candidate earns ≥$22,500/month fixed salary, which exempts them from COMPASS entirely).

Can I help with anything else on the application?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot provides comprehensive and accurate information covering both qualification verification (with detailed options beyond the reference) and workforce composition factors (C3 Diversity and C4 Local Employment) with specific point thresholds. It correctly explains that well-represented nationality yields fewer points and how local PMET share compares to sector benchmarks, fully addressing the user's question.

q-0050hard-13I'm renewing my Employment Pass under a sponsorship arrangement. The HR team mentioned that certain employer contributions to provident funds shouldn't be counted as part of my salary, and they also want to know what company financial details they need to provide on the renewal form. Can you tell me what the renewal form asks about the sponsor company's financials, and confirm whether provident fund contributions count toward salary?Based on the EP (Sponsorship) renewal form and salary guidance, I can answer both your questions: ## Sponsor Company Financial Details Required The renewal form asks for the following financial information about the **local sponsor company** (Part 3): 1. **Paid-up capital** (in S$) 2. **Nature of business** 3. **Company's turnover for the past 3 years**, including: - Year - Value (S$) - Whether the turnover figure is from an audited account (Yes/No) The form notes that for unaudited accounts or if the company is exempted from audit, you should select 'No' for the audit question. ## Provident Fund Contributions and Salary **Your HR team is correct.** Employer contributions to provident funds do **not** count toward your fixed monthly salary. Fixed monthly salary consists of: - **Basic monthly salary** (payment that doesn't vary month to month) - **Fixed monthly allowances** (monthly allowances that don't vary) The guidance explicitly excludes: - "Contributions payable by the employer to any pension or provident fund, including any contributions made on the employee's behalf" Also excluded are variable allowances, bonuses, commissions, overtime, stock options, reimbursements, and gratuity payments. Can I help with anything else about your EP renewal?The chatbot's answer accurately covers all required financial details (paid-up capital, nature of business, 3-year turnover with year/value/audit status) and correctly confirms that employer provident fund contributions are excluded from salary computation, matching the reference answer comprehensively.
Question:
I'm renewing my Employment Pass under a sponsorship arrangement. The HR team mentioned that certain employer contributions to provident funds shouldn't be counted as part of my salary, and they also want to know what company financial details they need to provide on the renewal form. Can you tell me what the renewal form asks about the sponsor company's financials, and confirm whether provident fund contributions count toward salary?
Expected:
Regarding the sponsor company's financials on the Employment Pass (Sponsorship) Renewal Form: the form requires the sponsor company's paid-up capital (in S$), the nature of business, and the company's turnover for the past 3 years. For each of the 3 years (starting with the most recent), the company must provide the year, the value of turnover (in S$), and indicate whether the turnover figure is from an audited account (selecting 'Yes' or 'No'; for unaudited accounts or if the employing company is exempted from audit, 'No' should be selected). Regarding provident fund contributions: any contributions payable by the employer to any pension or provident fund, including any contributions made on the foreign employee's behalf, are excluded from the computation of salary (i.e., they do not count as part of the foreign employee's fixed monthly salary).
Actual:
Based on the EP (Sponsorship) renewal form and salary guidance, I can answer both your questions:

## Sponsor Company Financial Details Required

The renewal form asks for the following financial information about the **local sponsor company** (Part 3):

1. **Paid-up capital** (in S$)
2. **Nature of business**
3. **Company's turnover for the past 3 years**, including:
   - Year
   - Value (S$)
   - Whether the turnover figure is from an audited account (Yes/No)

The form notes that for unaudited accounts or if the company is exempted from audit, you should select 'No' for the audit question.

## Provident Fund Contributions and Salary

**Your HR team is correct.** Employer contributions to provident funds do **not** count toward your fixed monthly salary.

Fixed monthly salary consists of:
- **Basic monthly salary** (payment that doesn't vary month to month)
- **Fixed monthly allowances** (monthly allowances that don't vary)

The guidance explicitly excludes:
- "Contributions payable by the employer to any pension or provident fund, including any contributions made on the employee's behalf"

Also excluded are variable allowances, bonuses, commissions, overtime, stock options, reimbursements, and gratuity payments.

Can I help with anything else about your EP renewal?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer accurately covers all required financial details (paid-up capital, nature of business, 3-year turnover with year/value/audit status) and correctly confirms that employer provident fund contributions are excluded from salary computation, matching the reference answer comprehensively.

Question:
We're looking to hire a clinical psychologist from overseas — the candidate is 38 years old. Expected:
For the COMPASS salary criterion at age 38, the required salary benchmarks are $8,530 to score 10 points (65th percentile of local PMETs) and $14,045 to score 20 points (90th percentile of local PMETs). These benchmarks are based on the candidate's age and represent the salary thresholds relative to local PMET pay in the sector.

For the Shortage Occupation List (SOL) skills bonus for a clinical psychologist, the eligible job title is 'Clinical psychologist'. The key job duties include assessing mental health and social needs, formulating care, and delivering a range of psychological therapies including Cognitive Behavioural Therapy across the age range and across hospital and community settings. More details on job duties can be found on MOH's Team Healthcare website.

The additional requirements are that applicants should have at least a Master's degree in Clinical Psychology. MOH will assess all applications and may contact employers for more information. The supporting sector agency is the Ministry of Health (MOH).

## COMPASS C1 Salary Benchmarks for Age 38

For a **38-year-old clinical psychologist** in the **Health & Social Services** sector:

- **10 points (65th percentile):** $8,530 per month
- **20 points (90th percentile):** $14,045 per month

These are the fixed monthly salary thresholds your candidate needs to meet to score points under the COMPASS C1 Salary criterion.

## C5 Skills Bonus Requirements for Clinical Psychologist

To qualify for the **COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus** (20 points) for the clinical psychologist shortage occupation, you need to meet these requirements:

**Eligible job title:**
- Clinical psychologist

**Key job duties:**
- Assess mental health and social needs, formulate care and deliver a range of psychological therapies including Cognitive Behavioural Therapy across the age range and across hospital and community settings

**Additional requirements:**
- **At least a Master's degree in Clinical Psychology** (this is mandatory)
- MOH will assess all applications and may contact employers for more information

**Supporting agency:** Ministry of Health (MOH)

- The job title you select in the EP application must match the occupation advertised on MyCareersFuture (unless you're exempt from FCF advertising)
- You'll need to upload the Master's degree in Clinical Psychology under the qualifications section of the EP application
- With the C5 Skills Bonus (20 points) and appropriate salary benchmarks, your candidate will have a strong COMPASS score

Question:
I'm about to renew an Employment Pass for one of our employees whose pass expires in October 2024. I also need to submit their educational qualification details as part of the application. Can you tell me what I need to know about the renewal criteria and how to handle the qualification entry if the institution name isn't showing up in the dropdown?
Expected:
For the renewal: Since the EP is expiring from 1 September 2024 onwards (which includes October 2024), the pass holder will need to pass the COMPASS requirements in order to renew.

For the educational qualification entry: If the institution name on the educational certificate or verification proof is not available in the dropdown menu, you should enter the institution's most recent name where applicable. You may also choose not to declare the qualification if you are unable to find it on the list. Additionally, you should enter the qualification level as per the verification proof or educational certificate, and enter the faculty as per the transcript if it is not found in the verification proof or educational certificate. You should also read the guidelines before submitting the verification proof.
Actual:
Based on the guidance, here's what you need to know about renewing an Employment Pass:

## **Renewal Criteria**

For passes expiring from 1 September 2024 onwards, your employee must meet these requirements:

1. **Qualifying salary**: At least the EP qualifying salary for their age and sector
2. **Same employer**: Continue working for you in a managerial, executive, or specialised role  
3. **COMPASS**: Pass the 40-point COMPASS assessment (unless earning at least $22,500 fixed monthly salary, which exempts them)

**Good news**: Once a qualification has been verified, there's no need to verify it again at renewal or when changing employers.

## **Renewal Timing & Process**

- You can renew **up to 6 months (180 days)** before the pass expires
- Any remaining validity (up to 6 months) from the current pass is added to the renewal
- Apply via the myMOM Portal
- Processing: within 10 business days

## **Handling Qualification Entry Issues**

If the institution name isn't showing up in the dropdown:

1. **Check the institution name carefully**:
   - Ensure it's correctly translated with no spelling errors
   - **Use the institution's most recent name** if it has undergone a name change
   - Select from the dropdown list of accredited institutions whenever possible

2. **If still not found**: You have the option to **not declare the qualification** if you cannot find it on the list, provided your employee doesn't need C2 points to pass COMPASS

3. **Enter information exactly as shown** on the certificate and verification proof when you do declare it

Since your employee's pass expires in October 2024, COMPASS will apply to this renewal. Make sure you've verified whether they need qualification points (C2) to reach the 40-point threshold before deciding whether to declare it.

Can I help with anything else about the renewal?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot correctly identifies COMPASS requirements for passes expiring from September 2024 onwards, and provides accurate guidance on handling missing institution names (use most recent name, option not to declare if not found). It also adds helpful context about renewal timing and process without contradicting the reference answer.

q-0053hard-12We're looking to bring in a process engineer for our semiconductor fab on an Employment Pass. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering. If the EP application gets rejected, can the candidate himself write in to MOM to appeal, or does it have to come from us as the employer? Also, are there any other requirements beyond the engineering degree that MOM checks for this particular role?For your EP appeal question: **only you as the employer** (or your appointed employment agent) may submit an appeal if the application is rejected. The candidate cannot appeal directly. You have 3 months from the rejection date to submit the appeal, and 85% are processed within 6 weeks. Regarding requirements for a process engineer role beyond the Bachelor's degree: **Stage 1 – Qualifying Salary** The candidate must meet the minimum monthly salary threshold based on age. Since you're applying in September 2026, the current thresholds apply (the higher thresholds kick in from 1 Jan 2027 for new applications). For the general sector (semiconductor manufacturing), the minimum is $5,600/month for candidates aged 23 or below, rising progressively with age up to $10,700/month for those aged 45 and above. **Stage 2 – COMPASS (40 points required)** The application must score at least 40 points across six criteria: 1. **C1 Salary** – how the offered salary compares to local PMET salaries in the manufacturing sector at that age (0, 10, or 20 points) 2. **C2 Qualifications** – the Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering will score 10 points (20 points only if from a top-tier institution on MOM's list) 3. **C3 Diversity** – based on the candidate's nationality share among your firm's PMETs (0, 10, or 20 points) 4. **C4 Support for Local Employment** – your firm's local PMET share relative to the manufacturing sector (0, 10, or 20 points) 5. **C5 Skills Bonus** – up to 20 points if the role is on the Shortage Occupation List 6. **C6 Strategic Economic Priorities Bonus** – 10 points if your company participates in eligible government programmes The degree alone won't guarantee approval – the combined COMPASS score and qualifying salary both matter. Can I help you check whether process engineers might qualify for the C5 skills bonus, or clarify any specific COMPASS criterion?Correctly states only employer can appeal, and provides extensive COMPASS framework details. However, misses the key SOL-specific requirements: (1) firm must be verified by EDB as being in semiconductor space, and (2) the specific degree requirements for process engineer role.
