Question:
We're looking to bring in a process engineer for our semiconductor fab on an Employment Pass. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering. If the EP application gets rejected, can the candidate himself write in to MOM to appeal, or does it have to come from us as the employer? Also, are there any other requirements beyond the engineering degree that MOM checks for this particular role?
Expected:
For the appeal: Only the employer or an authorised third party who submitted the EP application can make enquiries or appeals to MOM. MOM will not entertain enquiries or appeals from the candidate or anyone else, so the candidate cannot write in himself — it must come from you as the employer (or your authorised third party).

For the process engineer role under the shortage occupation list, the candidate must meet both of the following criteria:
(1) The candidate must be from a firm involved in the Semiconductor space. MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so no additional documentation is required from the employer. New entrants to the Semiconductor space can reach out to EDB.
(2) The candidate must have at least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering or science faculty. This includes, but is not limited to: Electrical Engineering, Mechanical Engineering, Chemical Engineering, Materials Engineering, and Material Sciences.

A Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering satisfies criterion (2), but criterion (1) — that the firm is in the semiconductor space — must also be met, and MOM will verify this with EDB directly.
Actual:
For your EP appeal question: **only you as the employer** (or your appointed employment agent) may submit an appeal if the application is rejected. The candidate cannot appeal directly. You have 3 months from the rejection date to submit the appeal, and 85% are processed within 6 weeks.

Regarding requirements for a process engineer role beyond the Bachelor's degree:

**Stage 1 – Qualifying Salary**  
The candidate must meet the minimum monthly salary threshold based on age. Since you're applying in September 2026, the current thresholds apply (the higher thresholds kick in from 1 Jan 2027 for new applications). For the general sector (semiconductor manufacturing), the minimum is $5,600/month for candidates aged 23 or below, rising progressively with age up to $10,700/month for those aged 45 and above.

**Stage 2 – COMPASS (40 points required)**  
The application must score at least 40 points across six criteria:

1. **C1 Salary** – how the offered salary compares to local PMET salaries in the manufacturing sector at that age (0, 10, or 20 points)
2. **C2 Qualifications** – the Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering will score 10 points (20 points only if from a top-tier institution on MOM's list)
3. **C3 Diversity** – based on the candidate's nationality share among your firm's PMETs (0, 10, or 20 points)
4. **C4 Support for Local Employment** – your firm's local PMET share relative to the manufacturing sector (0, 10, or 20 points)
5. **C5 Skills Bonus** – up to 20 points if the role is on the Shortage Occupation List
6. **C6 Strategic Economic Priorities Bonus** – 10 points if your company participates in eligible government programmes

The degree alone won't guarantee approval – the combined COMPASS score and qualifying salary both matter.

Can I help you check whether process engineers might qualify for the C5 skills bonus, or clarify any specific COMPASS criterion?

Correctly states only employer can appeal, and provides extensive COMPASS framework details. However, misses the key SOL-specific requirements: (1) firm must be verified by EDB as being in semiconductor space, and (2) the specific degree requirements for process engineer role. The user asked specifically about this role's requirements beyond the degree, and the reference answer's SOL criteria were not addressed.

q-0054hard-13I'm currently exploring my options between the ONE Pass and the Employment Pass. One thing I'm curious about — if I hold a ONE Pass and decide to switch to a different employer, do I need to reapply for a new pass? And separately, if my Employment Pass gets cancelled while I'm still in Singapore, how long can I stay on a Short-Term Visit Pass?Perfect! I can now answer both of your questions clearly. **ONE Pass – No need to reapply when changing jobs** If you hold a ONE Pass and switch employers, you do **not** need to reapply for a new pass. This is one of the key features of the ONE Pass — it gives you the flexibility to change jobs without going through another application process. You can even work for multiple companies concurrently. **Employment Pass – STVP duration after cancellation** When your Employment Pass is cancelled while you're still in Singapore, you can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) that grants you valid stay for **up to 90 days** from the date of application. You request this STVP through the EP eService when cancelling the pass, and both you and your employer will receive it by email. **Key difference**: The ONE Pass offers significantly more flexibility for career moves — you maintain the same pass regardless of employer changes, whereas with an EP, each new employer would need to apply for a new pass for you. Can I help you with anything else about these two pass types?The chatbot accurately answers both questions: ONE Pass holders don't need to reapply when changing employers and can work for multiple companies, and STVP grants up to 90 days after EP cancellation. The response covers the essential information from the reference answer.
Question:
I'm currently exploring my options between the ONE Pass and the Employment Pass. One thing I'm curious about — if I hold a ONE Pass and decide to switch to a different employer, do I need to reapply for a new pass? And separately, if my Employment Pass gets cancelled while I'm still in Singapore, how long can I stay on a Short-Term Visit Pass?
Expected:
If you hold a ONE Pass (Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass), you do not need to reapply for a pass if you change jobs. In fact, the ONE Pass provides the flexibility to concurrently start, operate, and work for multiple companies at any one time (subject to your employment contract terms, where relevant). It is also exempt from the Complementarity Assessment Framework (COMPASS) and Fair Consideration Framework job advertising requirements. Additionally, passes are available for eligible family members, and your spouse will be able to apply to work on a Letter of Consent. You may also apply for the ONE Pass without meeting the salary criteria if you have outstanding achievements in at least one specified area. Separately, for Employment Pass holders who are still in Singapore after their pass is cancelled, they can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) to grant them a valid stay for up to 90 days from the date of application.

When your Employment Pass is cancelled while you're still in Singapore, you can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) that grants you valid stay for **up to 90 days** from the date of application. You request this STVP through the EP eService when cancelling the pass, and both you and your employer will receive it by email.

q-0055hard-13We have an ONE Pass holder whose common-law spouse currently holds a Long-Term Visit Pass. The spouse has now received a job offer and wants to work here. Also, the ONE Pass holder's legally married spouse is on a Dependant's Pass and has also been offered a role — but she's actually been selected for an EP. Do both spouses need the same type of authorization to start working, and what do we need to do with the DP before the EP is issued?No, the two spouses need **different** types of authorization: **Common-law spouse on LTVP:** Needs a **Letter of Consent** to work. The prospective employer applies on their behalf, processing takes around 8 weeks, and the LTVP must have at least 3 months validity before applying. **Legally married spouse on Dependant's Pass:** Since she's been selected for an EP, she'll work under that **Employment Pass** — not a Letter of Consent. The EP gives her independent work and stay privileges. **What you must do with the DP:** You need to **cancel the Dependant's Pass before the EP is issued**. Once she holds an EP, her stay in Singapore no longer depends on the ONE Pass holder, so the DP is no longer required and must be cancelled first. Can I help with anything else regarding these applications?The chatbot's answer is substantively identical to the reference answer, correctly distinguishing the two authorization types (LOC for LTVP holder, EP for DP holder) and stating that the DP must be cancelled before the EP is issued.

q-0056hard-13I'm renewing my Employment Pass and I want to understand what gets verified during the process. I know MOM checks my income somehow, and I also recall having to deal with my university qualifications when I first applied. Can you explain how my income is verified with IRAS during renewal, and what I should do if my university's name doesn't appear in the dropdown when I need to submit educational verification proof?**Income verification with IRAS** Your income is verified automatically through a consent you give in your EP renewal declaration. When you renew, you consent for the Comptroller of Income Tax to verify your income stated in your application against your IRAS assessment record. IRAS checks your record for the current Year of Assessment; if that's not yet available or finalised, they verify against the two previous Years of Assessment instead. The results are then communicated directly to MOM's Controller of Work Passes. This happens behind the scenes—you don't need to submit tax documents yourself. **University not in the dropdown** If your university's name doesn't appear in the dropdown when submitting educational verification proof, enter the institution's **most recent name** where applicable. You also have the option to choose not to declare the qualification if you're unable to find it on the list. The guidance notes that the list of accredited institutions isn't exhaustive, so if your university isn't there, use its current official name as closely as possible. One important point: once your qualification has been verified during your initial application, you don't need to get it verified again at renewal or when changing employers. The system will only prompt you for verification if it's needed. Can I help with anything else about your EP renewal?Covers both income verification (consent, current YA or two previous YAs, results communicated to Controller of Work Passes) and university dropdown guidance (enter most recent name, option not to declare) accurately and comprehensively, matching the reference answer with helpful additional context.
Question:
I'm renewing my Employment Pass and I want to understand what gets verified during the process. I know MOM checks my income somehow, and I also recall having to deal with my university qualifications when I first applied. Can you explain how my income is verified with IRAS during renewal, and what I should do if my university's name doesn't appear in the dropdown when I need to submit educational verification proof?
Expected:
For income verification during EP renewal, the applicant consents to the Comptroller of Income Tax verifying the income stated in the current and renewal applications based on the assessment record for the current Year of Assessment. If that assessment record is not available or finalised at the point of verification, the Comptroller of Income Tax will verify income against the assessment records for the two previous Years of Assessment. The results of the verification are then communicated by the Comptroller of Income Tax to the Controller of Work Passes.

Regarding educational qualification verification: if the institution name on the educational certificate or verification proof is not available in the dropdown menu, the applicant should enter the institution's most recent name where applicable. Alternatively, the applicant may choose not to declare the qualification if they are unable to find it on the list. The applicant should also enter the qualification level as per the verification proof or educational certificate, and enter the faculty as per the transcript if it is not found in the verification proof or educational certificate.
Actual:
**Income verification with IRAS**

Your income is verified automatically through a consent you give in your EP renewal declaration. When you renew, you consent for the Comptroller of Income Tax to verify your income stated in your application against your IRAS assessment record. IRAS checks your record for the current Year of Assessment; if that's not yet available or finalised, they verify against the two previous Years of Assessment instead. The results are then communicated directly to MOM's Controller of Work Passes. This happens behind the scenes—you don't need to submit tax documents yourself.

**University not in the dropdown**

If your university's name doesn't appear in the dropdown when submitting educational verification proof, enter the institution's **most recent name** where applicable. You also have the option to choose not to declare the qualification if you're unable to find it on the list. The guidance notes that the list of accredited institutions isn't exhaustive, so if your university isn't there, use its current official name as closely as possible.

One important point: once your qualification has been verified during your initial application, you don't need to get it verified again at renewal or when changing employers. The system will only prompt you for verification if it's needed.

Can I help with anything else about your EP renewal?
Judge remark (scored 3):
Covers both income verification (consent, current YA or two previous YAs, results communicated to Controller of Work Passes) and university dropdown guidance (enter most recent name, option not to declare) accurately and comprehensively, matching the reference answer with helpful additional context.

Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose passport is expiring soon, and he also wants to bring his 22-year-old unmarried son to Singapore. Can I update the passport details via a written request to MOM, and can I apply for a Dependant's Pass for his son at the same time?
Expected:
For the passport update: You can update the passport number and expiry date instantly using the EP eService. Written requests to update these two details will no longer be accepted, so a written request will not work.

For the 22-year-old son: Passes for family members are not available for children who are married or over the age of 21. Since the son is 22, he is not eligible for a family pass under the ONE Pass, even though he is unmarried. He may choose to obtain his own work pass if he wishes to work in Singapore.

Additionally, note that although you can apply for family passes at the same time as the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, you may lose the application fees for each family pass if the ONE Pass application gets rejected.

Question:
We're hiring a digital forensics analyst who is 28 years old. We want to understand what salary benchmarks apply under COMPASS C1 for someone at that age, and also what qualifications or experience this role requires to qualify for the C5 skills bonus on the Shortage Occupation List.
Expected:
For a 28-year-old candidate, the COMPASS C1 salary benchmarks are: $8,600 to earn 10 points (65th percentile of local PMETs in ICT field) and $12,994 to earn 20 points (90th percentile of local PMETs in ICT field). For the C5 skills bonus under the Shortage Occupation List, the role of Digital forensics analyst is eligible, and the candidate must meet either of two requirements: (1a) at least a Bachelor's degree in one of the following faculties — Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies); OR (1b) a minimum total of three years' work experience in at least one of these job titles: Computer forensics expert, Computer forensics investigator, Cyber forensics expert, Cyber forensics investigation specialist, Cyber incident investigation specialist, Cyber threat investigation specialist, Digital forensic expert, Digital forensics analyst, ICT forensics expert, or Information forensics expert. The supporting agency for this SOL occupation is the Infocomm Media Development Authority (IMDA). The job duties include conducting complex post-incident forensic analysis to investigate causes of intrusion, attack, loss, or breach; identifying and defining forensic issues and root causes; developing reports detailing incident timeline, evidence, findings, conclusions and recommendations; supporting the design of a breach notification process after a security incident; selecting, testing, and deploying security products and collecting information about Internet-based criminal activities; and recommending corrective actions to prevent and mitigate internal control failures.
Actual:
For a **28-year-old digital forensics analyst**, here's what you need to know:

## COMPASS C1 Salary Benchmarks (Age 28)

The salary thresholds depend on your company's sector classification. For the **Info-communication Technology (ICT) sector**, which is most relevant for a digital forensics analyst:

- **10 points (65th percentile)**: $8,391/month
- **20 points (90th percentile)**: $12,764/month

These are the current benchmarks (released August 2025). From applications in 2026 onwards, the benchmarks are slightly higher: $8,600 for 10 points and $12,994 for 20 points.

If your company is classified under a different sector (e.g., Professional Services, Financial Services), the benchmarks will differ. You can check your sector classification online through MOM.

## C5 Skills Bonus – Digital Forensics Analyst

**Good news**: Digital forensics specialist **is on the Shortage Occupation List**, which means your candidate can qualify for the C5 Skills Bonus (20 points).

### Eligible Job Titles:
- Computer forensics expert
- Computer forensics investigator
- Cyber forensics expert
- Digital forensic expert
- **Digital forensics analyst** ✓
- ICT forensics expert
- Information forensics expert

### Key Requirements:

Your candidate must meet **either** (a) **or** (b):

**(a) Educational qualification:**
- At least a Bachelor's degree in:
  - Computer Science
  - Information Technology
  - Programming & Systems Analysis
  - Science (Computer Studies)

**(b) Work experience:**
- Minimum **3 years' total work experience** in any of the eligible job titles listed above (including digital forensics analyst)

### Application Notes:
- Select "Digital forensics analyst" (or the appropriate title) in the EP application form
- This must match the job title in your MyCareersFuture advertisement (unless you're exempt from advertising)
- Upload qualifications or declare work experience as required

Would you like help with anything else related to the EP application?

q-0059hard-13We have an ONE Pass holder who wants to bring over his 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, and also wants to start working in a restricted occupation. What type of pass can the son get, and what does the ONE Pass holder need to do before he can begin work in that restricted occupation?Based on the guidance: **For the 22-year-old son with a disability:** The son can get a **Long-Term Visit Pass**. Although he is over 21, he qualifies because he is an unmarried handicapped child aged 21 and above. Unmarried children under 21 would receive a Dependant's Pass, but handicapped children who are unmarried and 21 or older are eligible for the Long-Term Visit Pass instead. **For working in a restricted occupation:** The ONE Pass holder must **obtain approval from MOM before starting work** in the restricted occupation. They need to submit a request using MOM's online form, and can only begin work after the request has been approved. MOM will respond within 3 weeks in most cases. The restricted occupations are predominantly media-related roles (editors, journalists, producers, reporters) and religious roles (pastors, priests, monks, imams, rabbis, missionaries, and other religious workers). Is there anything else you'd like to know about the ONE Pass holder's situation?The answer correctly identifies Long-Term Visit Pass for the handicapped child over 21, explains the approval requirement before starting restricted occupation work, mentions the online form submission, and notes the 3-week response time. Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder who wants to bring over his 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, and also wants to start working in a restricted occupation. What type of pass can the son get, and what does the ONE Pass holder need to do before he can begin work in that restricted occupation?
Expected:
The 22-year-old unmarried handicapped child of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass (ONE Pass) holder is eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass (not a Dependant's Pass, which is only for unmarried children under 21). To work in a restricted occupation, the ONE Pass holder must submit a request to MOM using their online form (at go.gov.sg/request-restricted-occupation) before starting work in that occupation. The holder can only begin work in the restricted occupation after the request has been approved, and MOM will respond within 3 weeks in most cases. A full list of restricted occupations is available from MOM.

q-0060hard-13I'm applying for an Employment Pass and trying to understand the COMPASS bonus points. My company is wondering if we can get bonus points — we might qualify under the Shortage Occupation List for the C5 skills bonus, and we're also looking at the C6 SEP bonus. What do we need to do on both fronts to be eligible for these bonuses?For the **C5 Skills Bonus (Shortage Occupation List)**, your candidate must: 1. **Perform the key job duties** listed for a specific shortage occupation 2. **Select an eligible job title** in the EP application that matches the MyCareersFuture advertisement (unless you're exempt from advertising) 3. **Meet additional requirements** specific to that occupation, which typically include: - Relevant qualifications (Bachelor's degree in specific fields), OR - At least 3 years' work experience in related job titles - For certain sectors, your firm must be verified by the supporting agency (e.g., EDB for Agritech, Carbon Services, Semiconductor; MAS for Financial Services; MOH for Healthcare; IMDA for Tech roles) The SOL covers 29 occupations across sectors including Agritech, Financial Services, Carbon Services, Healthcare, Infocomm Technology, Maritime, and Semiconductors. Each has specific job duties and requirements detailed in the SOL Employer Guide. --- For the **C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) Bonus**, your organisation must participate in at least one eligible programme from these agencies: - **EDB**: DEI, Pioneer Certificate, RISC, or be a manufacturer with ≥500 local employees, or GTP participant - **EnterpriseSG**: Scale-Up SG, SGEP, or high-growth startups with ≥USD$10M investment over 36 months - **IMDA**: IMDA Accreditation or Spark Programme - **MPA**: Maritime Sector Incentive awards or MCF-BD recipients - **STB**: Selected BIF or STA grantees with innovative transformation projects - **NTUC**: Progressive firms with Company Training Committees or participating in government-supported programmes The supporting agencies verify eligibility directly with MOM—you don't need to submit additional documentation. It also provides additional helpful details about specific requirements and programmes without introducing errors.
Question:
I'm applying for an Employment Pass and trying to understand the COMPASS bonus points. My company is wondering if we can get bonus points — we might qualify under the Shortage Occupation List for the C5 skills bonus, and we're also looking at the C6 SEP bonus. What do we need to do on both fronts to be eligible for these bonuses?
Expected:
For the C5 Skills bonus (Shortage Occupation List), applicants should select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application form. This job title must be the same as the occupation used for the job advertisement in MyCareersFuture (MCF), unless the employer is exempted from the Fair Consideration Framework (FCF) job advertising requirement. For the C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) bonus, the organisation needs to participate in at least one of the eligible programmes. Specifically, firms must be supported by economic agencies or NTUC, and must participate in or meet the criteria for at least one of the specified programmes/categories to be considered for the SEP bonus.
Actual:
For the **C5 Skills Bonus (Shortage Occupation List)**, your candidate must:

1. **Perform the key job duties** listed for a specific shortage occupation
2. **Select an eligible job title** in the EP application that matches the MyCareersFuture advertisement (unless you're exempt from advertising)
3. **Meet additional requirements** specific to that occupation, which typically include:
   - Relevant qualifications (Bachelor's degree in specific fields), OR
   - At least 3 years' work experience in related job titles
   - For certain sectors, your firm must be verified by the supporting agency (e.g., EDB for Agritech, Carbon Services, Semiconductor; MAS for Financial Services; MOH for Healthcare; IMDA for Tech roles)

The SOL covers 29 occupations across sectors including Agritech, Financial Services, Carbon Services, Healthcare, Infocomm Technology, Maritime, and Semiconductors. Each has specific job duties and requirements detailed in the SOL Employer Guide.

---

For the **C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) Bonus**, your organisation must participate in at least one eligible programme from these agencies:

- **EDB**: DEI, Pioneer Certificate, RISC, or be a manufacturer with ≥500 local employees, or GTP participant
- **EnterpriseSG**: Scale-Up SG, SGEP, or high-growth startups with ≥USD$10M investment over 36 months
- **IMDA**: IMDA Accreditation or Spark Programme
- **MPA**: Maritime Sector Incentive awards or MCF-BD recipients
- **STB**: Selected BIF or STA grantees with innovative transformation projects
- **NTUC**: Progressive firms with Company Training Committees or participating in government-supported programmes

The supporting agencies verify eligibility directly with MOM—you don't need to submit additional documentation. Contact the relevant agency if you're unsure whether your company qualifies.

Can I help you with anything else about your EP application?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer covers all key points from the reference: selecting an eligible job title matching the MCF advertisement for C5, and participating in eligible programmes supported by economic agencies or NTUC for C6. It also provides additional helpful details about specific requirements and programmes without introducing errors.

## COMPASS C4 Points (Support for Local Employment)

With your local PMET share at the 35th percentile relative to your sector, your application will score **10 points** under C4. The scoring works as follows:

- **20 points**: 50th percentile and above
- **10 points**: 20th to less than 50th percentile ← *Your company is here*
- **0 points**: Below 20th percentile

Since you're at the 35th percentile, you fall into the middle band and will earn 10 points for this criterion.

q-0065hard-23We're putting together an EP application and trying to understand how COMPASS scoring works. Our candidate scores well on salary and qualifications but we're not sure about the firm-level criteria. Can you explain how the COMPASS points are structured — like what categories feed into the overall score and how they're grouped?COMPASS uses a six-criterion framework with a 40-point pass mark. The criteria are grouped into **individual attributes** (about the candidate) and **firm-related attributes** (about your company's workforce). **Individual attributes (C1 and C2):** - **C1 Salary** – Benchmarks the candidate's fixed monthly salary against local PMET salaries in your sector by age. Scores 0, 10, or 20 points depending on whether the salary falls below the 65th percentile, between the 65th and 90th, or at/above the 90th percentile. - **C2 Qualifications** – Awards 20 points for a degree from a top-tier institution, 10 points for other degree-equivalent qualifications, or 0 points if the candidate has no degree. Qualifications are optional unless you need the points to reach 40. **Firm-related attributes (C3 and C4):** - **C3 Diversity** – Measures what share of your firm's PMETs are the same nationality as the candidate. Lower concentration scores higher: less than 5% earns 20 points, 5–25% earns 10 points, 25% or more earns 0 points. Firms with fewer than 25 PMETs automatically score 10 points. - **C4 Support for local employment** – Compares your firm's local PMET share against your sector benchmark. Scores 20 points if you're at/above the 50th percentile, 10 points for the 20th to below 50th, or 0 points below the 20th. Firms with fewer than 25 PMETs automatically score 10 points. **Bonus criteria (C5 and C6):** - **C5 Skills bonus** – Up to 20 bonus points if the candidate is in a Shortage Occupation List role and meets the requirements (reduced to 10 points if the candidate's nationality forms one-third or more of your PMETs). - **C6 Strategic Economic Priorities bonus** – 10 bonus points if your firm participates in eligible government programmes. You can check your firm's C3 and C4 scores using the Workforce Insights tool on myMOM Portal—those scores are based on a rolling three-month average, so improving them takes time. Can I help with anything else on COMPASS?The chatbot accurately explains the COMPASS structure with all six criteria correctly grouped into Individual (C1, C2), Firm-Related (C3, C4), and Bonus (C5, C6) categories, includes the 40-point pass mark, and provides additional helpful detail about scoring thresholds and the Workforce Insights tool.
Question:
We're putting together an EP application and trying to understand how COMPASS scoring works. Our candidate scores well on salary and qualifications but we're not sure about the firm-level criteria. Can you explain how the COMPASS points are structured — like what categories feed into the overall score and how they're grouped?
Expected:
COMPASS requires an application to earn at least 40 points to pass. The points are structured into two groups: an "Individual score" and a "Firm-Related score." The Individual score is based on candidate-specific criteria — C1 (Salary) and C2 (Qualifications) — while the Firm-Related score is based on the employer's workforce profile — C3 (Diversity) and C4 (Support for Local Employment). Each of these four foundational criteria can award 0, 10, or 20 points. In addition, there are two bonus criteria: C5 (Skills Bonus for Shortage Occupation List, worth up to 20 points) and C6 (Strategic Economic Priorities Bonus, worth 10 points). The total across all criteria must reach at least 40 points for the application to pass COMPASS.
Actual:
COMPASS uses a six-criterion framework with a 40-point pass mark. The criteria are grouped into **individual attributes** (about the candidate) and **firm-related attributes** (about your company's workforce).

**Individual attributes (C1 and C2):**

- **C1 Salary** – Benchmarks the candidate's fixed monthly salary against local PMET salaries in your sector by age. Scores 0, 10, or 20 points depending on whether the salary falls below the 65th percentile, between the 65th and 90th, or at/above the 90th percentile.

- **C2 Qualifications** – Awards 20 points for a degree from a top-tier institution, 10 points for other degree-equivalent qualifications, or 0 points if the candidate has no degree. Qualifications are optional unless you need the points to reach 40.

**Firm-related attributes (C3 and C4):**

- **C3 Diversity** – Measures what share of your firm's PMETs are the same nationality as the candidate. Lower concentration scores higher: less than 5% earns 20 points, 5–25% earns 10 points, 25% or more earns 0 points. Firms with fewer than 25 PMETs automatically score 10 points.

- **C4 Support for local employment** – Compares your firm's local PMET share against your sector benchmark. Scores 20 points if you're at/above the 50th percentile, 10 points for the 20th to below 50th, or 0 points below the 20th. Firms with fewer than 25 PMETs automatically score 10 points.

**Bonus criteria (C5 and C6):**

- **C5 Skills bonus** – Up to 20 bonus points if the candidate is in a Shortage Occupation List role and meets the requirements (reduced to 10 points if the candidate's nationality forms one-third or more of your PMETs).

- **C6 Strategic Economic Priorities bonus** – 10 bonus points if your firm participates in eligible government programmes.

You can check your firm's C3 and C4 scores using the Workforce Insights tool on myMOM Portal—those scores are based on a rolling three-month average, so improving them takes time.

Can I help with anything else on COMPASS?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately explains the COMPASS structure with all six criteria correctly grouped into Individual (C1, C2), Firm-Related (C3, C4), and Bonus (C5, C6) categories, includes the 40-point pass mark, and provides additional helpful detail about scoring thresholds and the Workforce Insights tool.

Question:
My candidate graduated from a Singapore institution and we want to verify their qualification through an online portal instead of using a background screening company. I've heard there's a platform called OpenCerts that can be used. How exactly do we upload the certificate file on that platform, and what do we need to capture in the screenshot for the EP application?
Expected:

Actual:
Perfect! For OpenCerts verification, here's exactly what you need to do:

**How to upload the certificate on OpenCerts:**

1. Go to the OpenCerts portal (opencerts.io)
2. Drag and drop the digital certificate file, or click "Select File" to browse and upload it
3. The portal will verify the certificate and display whether it's authentic

**What to capture in your screenshot:**

You need to ensure the digital certificate has been verified as authentic through the OpenCerts portal, then upload the verified certificate file directly to the EP application.

**In the EP application form:**

1. Select the awarding institution from the dropdown
2. Enter the qualification level and faculty as shown on the certificate
3. Under "Verification method", select **"Digital certificate verified through OpenCerts portal"**
4. Upload the digital certificate file (OpenCerts only, max 2MB)

The key point is that the certificate must show as verified/authentic when you upload it to OpenCerts. You're uploading the actual digital certificate file to the EP application—not a screenshot of the OpenCerts verification page.

q-0068hard-23We posted a job ad on MyCareersFuture in early January and closed it after 30 days, so around early February. How soon after posting can we submit the EP application, and what's the deadline for using that same job ad?Based on the Fair Consideration Framework requirements: **When you can submit the EP application:** You can submit the EP application **15 days after posting** the job ad on MyCareersFuture. The ad must remain open for at least 14 consecutive days, so the earliest you can apply is on day 15. **Deadline for using the same job ad:** You have **3 months from when the ad closed** to use it for an EP application. Since you closed your ad in early February, you would have until early May to submit the EP application using that advertisement. After 3 months from closure, the ad expires and cannot be used for EP applications—you would need to post a new advertisement and keep it open for another 14 consecutive days. So in your case, if you posted in early January and closed after 30 days (early February), your window to submit the EP application using that ad would have been from mid-January (15 days after posting) until early May (3 months after closure). Can I help you with anything else regarding the EP application process?The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 15 days after posting to submit, ad must be open at least 14 consecutive days, 3 months from closing date to use the ad, and correctly applies these rules to the user's scenario (mid-January to early May window).
Question:
We posted a job ad on MyCareersFuture in early January and closed it after 30 days, so around early February. How soon after posting can we submit the EP application, and what's the deadline for using that same job ad?
Expected:
You can submit the EP or S Pass application at the earliest 15 days after posting the job ad. Once the job ad closes (in this example, after 30 days around early February), you have 3 months from the closing date to submit the EP or S Pass application using that job ad. After that 3-month window (in this example, around early May), the advertisement can no longer be used and you would need to post a new one. Additionally, the job ad must be open for at least 14 consecutive days before you can submit the application.
Actual:
Based on the Fair Consideration Framework requirements:

**When you can submit the EP application:**
You can submit the EP application **15 days after posting** the job ad on MyCareersFuture. The ad must remain open for at least 14 consecutive days, so the earliest you can apply is on day 15.

**Deadline for using the same job ad:**
You have **3 months from when the ad closed** to use it for an EP application. Since you closed your ad in early February, you would have until early May to submit the EP application using that advertisement.

After 3 months from closure, the ad expires and cannot be used for EP applications—you would need to post a new advertisement and keep it open for another 14 consecutive days.

So in your case, if you posted in early January and closed after 30 days (early February), your window to submit the EP application using that ad would have been from mid-January (15 days after posting) until early May (3 months after closure).

For the Fund Management Activities & Activities Auxiliary to Financial Services sector, a candidate aged 30 needs a fixed monthly salary of at least $13,030 to score 10 points under C1 (meeting the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries), and at least $21,401 to score 20 points (meeting the 90th percentile of local PMET salaries). These benchmarks are from the release dated August 2025 and are derived from MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department's (MRSD) annual Comprehensive Labour Force Survey. The required salary increases progressively with age, from age 23 up to age 45. For this sector, the range starts at $8,917 (10 points) / $12,474 (20 points) for candidates aged 23 or below, and goes up to $21,845 (10 points) / $40,530 (20 points) for candidates aged 45 or above. The benchmarks are published by the Ministry of Manpower (MOM). Note that candidates who do not meet the EP qualifying salary will not be eligible for an EP regardless of COMPASS points, and EP candidates earning at least $22,500 in fixed monthly salary are exempted from COMPASS entirely.

q-0070hard-22I'm about to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate we're hiring. I want to make sure I'm submitting through the right government body — which ministry handles EP applications in Singapore?The Ministry of Manpower (MOM) handles Employment Pass applications in Singapore. You'll submit your application through MOM's EP eService online. The application is processed within 10 business days for most cases. You'll pay a $105 application fee when you submit, and once approved, you'll receive an In-Principle Approval (IPA) letter. The IPA is valid for 6 months as a single-entry visa for your candidate to enter Singapore and get the pass issued. Question:
I'm about to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate we're hiring. I want to make sure I'm submitting through the right government body — which ministry handles EP applications in Singapore?
Expected:
Employment Pass applications in Singapore are handled by the Ministry of Manpower (MOM). The application fee is $105, and for Singapore-registered companies, applications are processed or given an update within 10 business days. For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company, processing takes within 6 weeks for most cases.

q-0071hard-23We're preparing a Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder. The applicant will be partially paid by both a local and an overseas employer. When filling in the salary section of the LOC form, how exactly does MOM define 'fixed monthly salary', and what happens if the fixed monthly salary amount we declare is higher than the basic monthly salary?For your Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder, here's how MOM defines fixed monthly salary: **Fixed monthly salary = Basic monthly salary + Fixed monthly allowances** - **Basic monthly salary**: Payment that does not vary month to month, regardless of employee or company performance, and regardless of whether the employee takes medical or personal leave. - **Fixed monthly allowances**: Monthly allowances that do not vary month to month (e.g., fixed food and housing allowances). **What happens if fixed monthly salary is higher than basic monthly salary:** When you declare a fixed monthly salary amount that is higher than the basic monthly salary, MOM will automatically treat the difference as "fixed monthly allowances." The LOC application form explicitly states: *"If the amount indicated as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will take the difference as the 'fixed monthly allowances'."* **Important note:** If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the fixed monthly salary should be exactly the same as the basic monthly salary. **What fixed monthly salary excludes:** - Variable allowances - Overtime, bonus, commission, or annual wage supplements - Board fees, stock options, or dividends - In-kind payments - Reimbursements - Productivity incentive payments - Employer pension/provident fund contributions - Gratuity payments Since your applicant will be partially paid by both local and overseas employers, make sure to select "Both local and overseas" in section 4C of the form and declare the total combined fixed monthly salary from both sources. Can I help you with anything else regarding the LOC application?The chatbot accurately defines fixed monthly salary as basic monthly salary plus fixed monthly allowances, correctly explains that MOM treats the difference as fixed monthly allowances when fixed monthly salary exceeds basic monthly salary, notes that they should be equal if there are no allowances, and correctly addresses the local/overseas employer payment option. The additional details about exclusions are helpful extras, not noise.
Question:
We're preparing a Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder. The applicant will be partially paid by both a local and an overseas employer. When filling in the salary section of the LOC form, how exactly does MOM define 'fixed monthly salary', and what happens if the fixed monthly salary amount we declare is higher than the basic monthly salary?
Expected:
According to the LOC (Common-Law Spouse) Application Form, fixed monthly salary is defined as basic monthly salary plus fixed monthly allowances. For example, S$5,000 fixed monthly salary = S$4,500 basic monthly salary + S$500 fixed monthly allowances. MOM will use the fixed monthly salary to assess the application. If the amount indicated as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will take the difference as the 'fixed monthly allowances'. If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the amount of fixed monthly salary should be exactly the same as the basic monthly salary. For the salary payable by, there are three options: 'Local' (paid by a Singapore-registered employer or paid by an overseas employer which is then charged fully to a Singapore-registered employer), 'Overseas' (employer is not registered in Singapore or is a representative office registered with Enterprise Singapore), or 'Both local and overseas' (partially paid by both local and overseas employer).
Actual:
For your Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder, here's how MOM defines fixed monthly salary:

**Fixed monthly salary = Basic monthly salary + Fixed monthly allowances**

- **Basic monthly salary**: Payment that does not vary month to month, regardless of employee or company performance, and regardless of whether the employee takes medical or personal leave.

- **Fixed monthly allowances**: Monthly allowances that do not vary month to month (e.g., fixed food and housing allowances).

**What happens if fixed monthly salary is higher than basic monthly salary:**

When you declare a fixed monthly salary amount that is higher than the basic monthly salary, MOM will automatically treat the difference as "fixed monthly allowances." 

The LOC application form explicitly states: *"If the amount indicated as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will take the difference as the 'fixed monthly allowances'."*

**Important note:** If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the fixed monthly salary should be exactly the same as the basic monthly salary.

**What fixed monthly salary excludes:**
- Variable allowances
- Overtime, bonus, commission, or annual wage supplements
- Board fees, stock options, or dividends
- In-kind payments
- Reimbursements
- Productivity incentive payments
- Employer pension/provident fund contributions
- Gratuity payments

Since your applicant will be partially paid by both local and overseas employers, make sure to select "Both local and overseas" in section 4C of the form and declare the total combined fixed monthly salary from both sources.

Can I help you with anything else regarding the LOC application?
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately defines fixed monthly salary as basic monthly salary plus fixed monthly allowances, correctly explains that MOM treats the difference as fixed monthly allowances when fixed monthly salary exceeds basic monthly salary, notes that they should be equal if there are no allowances, and correctly addresses the local/overseas employer payment option. The additional details about exclusions are helpful extras, not noise.
